## Session 146 — Player fix follow-up: warm episodes + fallback failover (2026-11-12 ~08:05 PKT)

**What:** verification of the v1.1.0 player fix (main 88019eccc) against the 12-hour plan's GOAL 1, on branch `muse/player-fix-20261112`. The claimed fix is present and correct for the movie / best-print path: `?warm=1` + preferred audio on detail open (`DetailViewModel.loadDetail` -> `CatalogRepository.getPostDetail` -> `RaddStreamApi.getPostDetail`), server-side warm banks `resolved_cache` and `/api/v1/stream` 302s from cache (`viewer.py:_preresolve_for_detail`, `_resolve_and_bank`, stream-handler cache lookup), audio-first + gated-last ladder (`viewer.py:_option_groups`), client pre-resolve (`preresolveTopLink`, 2 s ceiling, 120 s reuse), 8 s watchdog + failover machine + 10 s HTTP timeouts. Three remaining gaps found by reading the code, all on the seasons/episodes path: **(1) Episodes are never warmed** — the server warms only the first option group and the client pre-resolves only `bestPrintOf`, so an episode Play tap always pays its 5-11 s live resolve (Salaar 720p 9.25 s) inside the player. Fix: `showRungsFor` now warms the opened scope's Auto handle in the background via `GET /api/v1/stream?redirect=0` (`warmScopeLink`: one in-flight warm, cancelled on scope change, 30 s ceiling, silent on failure, deduped per handle; pure handle pick `scopeWarmHandle` in `DetailPreResolve.kt`). **(2) The playLink fallback staged no failover** — `playPrint`'s fallback launched a bare single-rendition bundle, so the watchdog could only retry the same slow address and then give up while sibling copies sat unused in `print.links`. Fix: `playLink` takes an optional `backupHandles` list and stages it when non-empty (never with an empty list, so `playV2Bundle`'s staged runner-ups are never wiped); the fallback passes `siblingBackupHandles(print, link)` (other playable, non-page copies, capped at 5). **(3) The v2 work path warmed without audio** — `getWorkDetailV2`'s bridge call sent `warm=1` with no `audio`, so the warmed auto tier could be a different audio than the ladder Play presses. Fix: `audio = detailWarmAudio(userRegion)` threaded through `getWorkDetailV2`. Deliberately NOT changed: the 8 s watchdog / 10 s HTTP timeouts / 2 s playV2 ceiling (tuned constants); known tension recorded in `hidden_files/player-fix-diagnosis-20261112.md` — a cold 9.25 s resolve exceeds the 8 s watchdog, so a cold instant tap can still be cut off before its first resolve lands, and the retry-same races the server-side banking (retry ~8.8 s vs bank ~9.25 s). No `viewer.py` change (backend agent owns it), no live-tree / DB touch, no merge, no tag.

**Checks:** duplicate top-level type grep over the whole App tree (to run after commit); new tests in `PreResolveTest` (scope-warm handle: auto rung / download-only auto -> null / empty ladder -> null; sibling backups: other playable copies / page + dead excluded / dedupe + cap 5 / lone copy -> empty). No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict; merge to main (coordinator's call); **the player fix is still UNVERIFIED on a real device — a green CI run proves the code compiles and the unit tests pass, not that a video plays; only the coordinator's emulator-loop device test (a frame playing for a movie AND an episode) counts.**

## Hero Watch Now fix — Appetize v1.1.2 FAILED 3 (2026-11-12 ~15:30 PKT)

**What:** the hero carousel's "Watch Now" did nothing on two taps (carousel kept rotating) while tapping the banner image opened the detail page. Root cause, found by reading the layout, not by guessing at the race: the page dots sat at the BOTTOM end, in the buttons' strip (bottom 20dp, buttons 52dp tall, dots' 48dp hit boxes [20,68] vs buttons [20,72] from the bottom), declared after the pager so on top in z-order. On a 6-dot hero the dots row spans x 97–395dp on a 411dp Pixel 6/7 and covers the Watch Now button's centre (~107dp) — a centred tap hit a dot (re-selecting the slide: no visible change) instead of the button. My List was overlapped at every dot count ≥ 2. Fix in `HeroCarousel.kt` only: (1) the indicator moves to the TOP end in a 0.55f-surface contrast pill — no dot can cover any control at any dot count, and the pill keeps the dots readable over bright artwork where the top scrim is clear; (2) the auto-advance loop now also skips its tick while a finger is down on Watch Now (`watchNowPressed`, reported up from the slide's own interaction source — deliberately NOT a LaunchedEffect key, so an in-flight advance is never cancelled mid-flight leaving the pager stranded between pages), so an advance starting mid-press can't slide the button away and cancel the tap; (3) the advance step is the pure `nextHeroPage` (wrap-around). The `onWatchNow` lambda already captures the composed page's post at tap time, so no stale-item change was needed; banner-image tap, dot taps and auto-rotation are untouched in behaviour. New tests in `HeroCarouselTest`: `nextHeroPage` advance/wrap/degenerate, `indicatorBandClearsTheButtonStrip` (320dp + 400dp heroes), and a witness that the old bottom-anchored placement overlapped the strip. Button/indicator geometry now lives in shared constants (`HERO_INDICATOR_TOP_DP`, `HERO_BUTTON_HEIGHT_DP`, `HERO_ACTION_BOTTOM_DP`) so the test guards the real layout.

## Session 147 — Appetize FAILED 2 + FAILED 1: detail first-load retry + Salaar play (2026-11-12 ~15:26–17:00 PKT)

**What:** app-fix builder for the two Appetize v1.1.2 failures, on branch `muse/appfix-detail-salaar-20261112` from main `199e8bc2`. **FAILED 2 (first):** detail pages failed on FIRST load ("Failed to load details. Check connection.") and loaded fully on Retry, 100% reproducible. Measured from the Oracle VM: the v2 detail chain for Salaar (v2 id 74537, v1 post 79881) costs 6.5–10 s server-side — the family work-ref lookup is a 4.8 s full-table scan (`_resolve_work_ref`, Python UDF per row) and `catalog_rev`'s `MAX(source_rev)` is a 0.25–0.37 s full scan of 197,530 `cat_works` rows on EVERY v2 GET. Server fix: `idx_posts_year ON posts(year)` and `idx_cat_works_source_rev ON cat_works(source_rev)` in `fast_fetch.init_db` + `catalog_v2.py` DDL (rebuilds mint them too); the `cat_works` index is guarded on table AND column — a stub table without `source_rev` skips it instead of breaking `init_db` (caught by the suite run, fixed before commit). **FAILED 1:** Salaar's default version did not play — but the API can play it: 720p auto, 480p and sd handles all pressed 302 from the VM. Mechanism: a Play press during the ~9.25 s daemon warm starts a SECOND cold resolve, the 8 s watchdog fires, retry starts a THIRD, failover a FOURTH — ~30 s of parallel cold resolves ending in `NO_CANDIDATE_LEFT`. Server fix: `viewer.py:_resolve_coalesced` (keyed by `(source_id, v2, want)`, 12 s wait bound) used by the stream handler, download handler and daemon warm; a timed-out waiter falls back to its own resolve. App fix: **`loadDetail` auto-retries** (`fetchDetailWithRetry`): up to 3 attempts, backoff 600 ms → 1.5 s, only `IOException` and `HttpException` 429/5xx, plus one retry of a null detail (the v2 path swallows HTTP/IO failures into null); `CancellationException` rethrown (was swallowed). **`playV2` ceilings** 2 s → 10 s (background pre-resolve) / 6 s (Play-press call) — the old ceiling timed out healthy calls under load; neither call resolves a link, so playback cannot stall on them; watchdog 8 s unchanged. **Scope-warm dedup wart:** `lastScopeWarmHandle` set only on warm success. Pure policy (`isTransientDetailError`, `detailLoadBackoffMs`, constants) in `DetailPreResolve.kt`, 5 new tests in `PreResolveTest`. No live-tree / DB touch, no service restart, no tag, no release.

**Checks:** full import diff per edited Kotlin file (run after commit — script compares main...HEAD); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree: 581 declarations, 0 duplicates. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdicts; merge to main (coordinator's call); **still UNVERIFIED on a real device — Appetize minutes are exhausted, so the device proof (first-load detail on 3 titles incl. Salaar + McLintock; Salaar default-version Play pressed within seconds of detail load) is for the TestMu AI retest.**
## Session 148 — Salaar won't play on ANY version: /play mistranslation + mint-gate table (2026-11-12 ~16:30–17:15 PKT)

**What:** app-fix builder for ISSUE 1 (GOAL 1), on branch `muse/salaar-play-20261112` from main `ee5be6f3` (appfix merged). The Samsung RTL test (v1.1.2, 2 Galaxy phones) showed Salaar failing on every version with a "Cache engine failed" toast, while the API demonstrably plays it. Root-caused live, not from the code alone. **RC1 — `/play` mistranslates v2-path cat ids as legacy ids (wrong title):** the app's v2 detail maps prints verbatim (`WireDetailPrint.releaseId` is a cat id — Salaar 4K is 1318138, while the same title's v1 legacy ids are 1229325+, zero overlap), and `route_play` translated every bare `release_id` through `cat_id_map`. Proven: `{"release_id": 1318138}` returned The Strangers Chapter 2 (work 76848), whose winner handle presses 404; `{"release_id": 1318132}` (Salaar top 720p) returned work 84058, `download_only`; McLintock cat 1576938 also collides (work 112129). Control: legacy `{"release_id": 1229326}` returned Salaar work 74537, playable, 2 stream candidates. The server cannot disambiguate (both universes share numbers); only the caller knows. Fix: `WirePlayRequest.legacyReleaseId` (`legacy_release_id`, null = default legacy), `CatalogRepository.playV2(..., legacyReleaseId = true)`, `DetailViewModel` passes `workId < 0` at both `playV2` call sites (pre-resolve AND Play-press — the pre-resolve matters most, since a mistranslated warm would cache the wrong title's bundle for Play to reuse); `route_play` honors the flag (default true). **RC2 — the mint gate reads the wrong table:** `_bundle_is_gated` queried legacy `releases` with the bundle's cat id (always a cat id — `play_bundle` translates at entry), so a gated 4K print minted handles for a free caller. Proven: legacy 1229331 → playable 2160p bundle whose candidate handle presses 402. Fix: read `cat_releases` (resolution/quality_label/codec/label via `tiers.band_of`; size_bytes deliberately NOT passed — a byte count can contain "1080"/"720" spuriously). **App step-down:** `playPrint` fell back to the same gated print's own link when `playV2` yielded nothing — that link 402s, and the player burned its whole watchdog budget on copies that cannot play. Fix: for a premium print with no premium access, step down once to the best allowed print (pure `stepDownPrint` in `DetailPreResolve.kt`, prefers a playable copy) instead of pressing the gated link. **(b) "Cache engine failed":** symptom, not a cause — the string exists once (`CacheAffordance` maps `CacheState.FAILED` to the Keep chip text); the detail-open preview seed fetches the winner handle, a 402/404 handle fails the task, the chip reports it. `PlaybackService.localCopyUrlFor` treats engine failure as silence and streams from the network, so it cannot block playback; fixed handles make the chip stop failing. No separate change. **(c) "Try another version":** `startStreamHandle` builds a single-rendition bundle, so `tryNextPrint` can only hop within the same print's staged runner-ups — it never re-resolves a different print. After RC1+RC2 the bundle is the right title with live runner-ups, so the control works as designed; no player restructuring. **(d) 502s:** transient nginx upstream drops (did not reproduce on re-press — all three top-print handles 402 that run); `RenditionFailover` already retries-then-hops on any player error including `ERROR_CODE_IO_BAD_HTTP_STATUS`. No separate change. Contributing, already merged pending live deploy: the cold-resolve (9–12 s) vs 8 s watchdog race (`ba61135d` coalescing, `ee5be6f3`).

**Checks:** backend suites green locally (`nice -n 19`): `test_api_v2` 76 (74 + 2 new: cat id with `legacy_release_id: false` returns the named print despite a colliding map row; gate refuses a 1080p cat print even with a 720p legacy decoy row at the same id), `test_catalog_v2` 105, `test_edge_phase1` 19, `test_redact` 96. New app tests: `V2PlayWireTest` (universe flag serializes false/true/omitted), `PreResolveTest` step-down (best allowed print / null when none / null for premium caller / 480p cap demotes 720p). Full import diff per edited Kotlin file + duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (to run after commit — script compares main...HEAD). No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdicts; merge to main (coordinator's call); **still UNVERIFIED on a real device — only the Samsung retest proves Salaar plays on default Play and via Try-another-version.**



## Samsung RTL UI fixes — Issues 3/4/5/9 (2026-11-12 ~16:30 PKT)

**What:** four Samsung RTL device-test issues (v1.1.2, 2 real Galaxy phones), built in the fresh clone `~/workspace/appfix-ui-20261112` on branch `muse/appfix-ui-20261112` from main `0d1f4559`. **Issue 3 (hero blank grey ~10 s + rotation too fast):** measured first — the backend is fast from the Oracle VM (`/api/v1/posts` 0.04 s, `sort=trending` 0.05 s, `/api/v2/browse` 0.79 s, `/api/v1/art` 0.04 s), so no endpoint blocks the hero; the ~10 s is device-side cold start + transit + the six backdrop downloads, and the hero showed it as flat grey because the skeleton's shimmer band (0.16f) is near-invisible over the surfaceVariant base. Fix: auto-advance 5 s → 8 s in all four default sites (`HeroCarousel.AUTO_ADVANCE_MS`, `HomeScreen.heroIntervalMs`, `PanelTheme.heroIntervalS`, theme-mirror fallback in `ServerSettingsRepository`; panel can still publish any value); the advance loop now ticks every 500 ms and advances only when a full interval has passed since the last page change or user interaction — dot tap, drag start, and page change all restart the clock (pure `shouldAdvanceHero`, tested) — while keeping the press/scroll guards; `HeroSkeleton` gains a stronger shimmer band (`highlightAlpha` param on `ShimmerBox`, 0.30f for the hero) plus the brand mark, so the wait reads as loading, not a blank slot. **Issue 4 (disclaimer text overlaps/clips):** rewrote `DisclaimerDialog` from `AlertDialog` (unbounded scrolling Column in the text slot) to a `Dialog` + `Surface` with a fixed title, a `weight(1f)` scrollable text area inside a dialog capped at 85% of screen height, and buttons pinned in their own bottom row — no text can reach the buttons at any text length (panel allows 4000 chars) or font scale; first-run stays non-dismissable (back/outside disabled), Settings stays dismissable. Same signature, both call sites untouched. **Issue 5 (Salaar poster shows dark "SA" placeholder):** investigated first — the API's poster URL (`https://image.tmdb.org/t/p/w500/qsnsKf59pYN3GzfQRqvNjE8tcdz.jpg`, work id 4220 / post 79881, year 2023 in data — the test's "2024" is the report's year, not the catalog's) HEADs 200 in 0.19 s from the Oracle VM, so it is an APP issue, not data: Coil's single attempt left one transient failure as the fallback for the whole visit. Fix in `CatalogImage` (the one loader for every poster): a failed load re-requests at once up to 2 times (pure `shouldAutoRetryImage` bound), and once spent the fallback tile itself is tappable to retry (unbounded — a tap is deliberate). Immediate retries on purpose: a delayed retry would park a coroutine on the composition clock, which the frozen-clock Robolectric tests cannot advance past. No wifi icon exists in the poster/fallback path — the dark tile is `ImageFallbackCard` (brand mark + "SA" initials); the mark is presumably what read as a wifi icon. **Issue 9 (landscape detail Play button off the right edge, system nav overlaps):** the Scaffold's padding covers status/navigation bars but not the display cutout, which sits mid-height on one side in landscape. New pure `detailContentPadding` takes the larger of the Scaffold's ask and the safe-drawing inset per side (never double-counted; portrait unchanged) plus the 40 dp bottom breathing room (P-22), applied to the detail content, skeleton and error state; the TopAppBar also clears the cutout horizontally so the back arrow never sits under the hole.

**Checks:** full import diff per edited Kotlin file (script compares main...HEAD, run after commit); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree — new names (`shouldAdvanceHero`, `shouldAutoRetryImage`/`MAX_IMAGE_AUTO_RETRIES`, `detailContentPadding`, `CatalogImageRetryTest`, `DetailContentPaddingTest`, `DisclaimerDialogTest`) each declared once; the remaining hits are pre-existing regex false positives (extension receivers, overloads, `fun interface`) in untouched files. New tests: `shouldAdvanceHero` timing cases in `HeroCarouselTest`, `CatalogImageRetryTest`, `DetailContentPaddingTest`, `DisclaimerDialogTest` (long-text dialog keeps both buttons displayed); updated test: `VipRemoteConfigTest` default interval 5 → 8. Pure-function expectations verified by a mirror script before commit. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. Deliberately NOT touched: player/resolve, detail-load, search-filter, dialog-dismiss, navigation-controller code (parallel builders own Issues 1 and 6+7+8); no live-tree / DB touch, no service restart, no tag, no release. **Done (merge agent, rebase onto main b9722a4c):** pushed; CI run 43212376360 on 5ccd72c9 completed/success, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, full unit suite green (independent verification ~/workspace/goals/radd-stream-on-oracle-vm/hidden_files/appfix-ui-verification-20261112.md). Merge to main: coordinator's call; nothing verified on a device — the Samsung retest (12 credits left) proves the visuals.

**Fixup 1 (CI run 43211237749, commit ed0e195f):** `:app:compileDebugKotlin FAILED` with one root error — `calculateStartPadding` / `calculateEndPadding` are unresolved in this Compose version (BOM 2024.06.00 / foundation-layout 1.7 has only the left/right members); the `maxOf` "no candidate" errors were consequential (unresolved argument type). Fix: `detailContentPadding` resolves start/end through `calculateLeftPadding` / `calculateRightPadding` with the layout direction (LTR start = physical left, RTL start = physical right), and `DetailContentPaddingTest` asserts left/right instead. No other file used those two functions.
## Samsung RTL retest — Issues 6/7/8: search filter, player dialog, Home tab (2026-11-12 ~16:30 PKT)

**What:** three interaction/state fixes from the Samsung RTL device test of v1.1.2 (2 real Galaxy phones), branch `muse/appfix-nav-search-20261112` from main `0d1f4559`. **Issue 6 — Series filter unreliable:** two defects in `SearchViewModel`. (1) `resetFilters()` kept the Type chip (`SearchFilters(type = it.filters.type)`), so the empty state's "Clear filters" button and the sheet's "Reset" re-ran the same Series-filtered search and the list stayed at "No matches" — the button looked dead. Fix: reset builds a default `SearchFilters()` (sort to default too). (2) Stale-job overwrite race: `Job.cancel()` only bites at the next suspension point, so a search whose network call already returned kept running through its non-suspending write — a slow earlier search overwrote the newer one's list, so the same query + Series filter answered 1 result once and "No matches" on repeats, and a tap on the result card was followed by the stale job landing and the list going empty. Fix: a monotonic `searchGeneration`, bumped on every schedule (fresh searches also cancel a pending `loadMore`); every write after the debounce, after each v2/v1 call, and in the failure path bails unless still the latest generation; `loadMore` captures its generation and bails the same way; `scheduleSuggest` drops chips when the query has moved on. The card tap itself navigates correctly (`PosterCard` → `onPostClick` → `navigateToPost`) — the "re-ran the search" was the stale job landing after the tap. Live probes (read-only, public API): v1 `type=series` and v2 `type=series` both answer series titles (Breaking Bad 6 vs 9, Squid Game 5 vs 23 — v2 counts works, v1 counts posts); the routes disagree only in that v1 matches `content_type='series'` exactly while v2 folds complete/ongoing seasons into `series` — a real inconsistency, but NOT the flip cause, so no backend change: noted as a follow-up (one clause in `viewer.py:_facet_filter_clauses`) to keep this diff app-only and conflict-free. **Issue 7 — player dialog "Back to details" didn't dismiss:** two player-side fixes in `PlayerScreen.kt`. (1) The tap only popped: clearing the card's own state first (`vm.dismissGiveUp()` + `vm.dismissDeadlineGiveUp()`) then popping, via a shared `backToDetails` lambda used by both GiveUpCard call sites (handoff + 30 s deadline), so the tap is visibly answered at once whatever the pop does — including when the Detail underneath re-pushes the player on return. (2) The player forces landscape, so the dialog is short: up to six stacked 48dp actions + icon/title/text can exceed a ~360dp landscape screen and push "Back to details" / "Keep waiting" off the bottom edge, where a tap hits the scrim and (dismissOnClickOutside=false) does nothing. Fix: the actions stack scrolls inside a 216dp cap (`GiveUpActionsMaxHeight`). Also found while investigating (NOT fixed here — DetailScreen belongs to the parallel detail-load builder): Detail's autoplay effect keys on `(post.id, resumeLoaded, autoplay)` with `autoPlayedFor` as `remember` state, which resets when Detail leaves composition — so an autoplay Detail re-plays on EVERY return from the Player; needs a ViewModel-held flag, routed to the coordinator. **Issue 8 — Home tab didn't switch from Search:** the tap was gated on the composition-time route snapshot (`currentRoute`); after failed playback cycles the snapshot can lag the back stack and a stale read turns every tap into a silent no-op. Fix in `NavGraph.kt`: the tap reads `navController.currentDestination?.route` live at tap time; the navigate block (popUpTo Home + saveState + launchSingleTop + restoreState) is unchanged, and the selected highlight still reads the composed route. Honest caveat: no single deterministic NavController defect was provable from code alone — the navigate block itself is standard and correct in every back-stack shape traced; the stale-snapshot gate and the stuck-modal paths (Issue 7's dialog; the filter sheet's scrim eats the first tap while open) are the two tap-swallowing paths removed here. If the retest still fails with no sheet/dialog open, it needs a device repro of the NavController state.

**Checks:** full import diff per edited Kotlin file (no imports added or removed in any edited file — `dp`/`verticalScroll`/`rememberScrollState`/`heightIn` were already imported in PlayerScreen.kt); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree: `SearchFilterStateTest`, `NavTabHomeTest`, `GiveUpActionsMaxHeight` each declared once, no new top-level type in the edited main files; brace/paren balance verified on all edited + new files after stripping strings/comments; new-test assertions verified against the edited sources with a mirror script before commit (resetFilters body, generation-check counts 4/2/1, back-to-details lambda ×2, no `onBackToDetails = onBackClick` left, live-read gate present + old gate absent). New tests: `SearchFilterStateTest` (reset clears type, generation guards), `NavTabHomeTest` (live-destination tap, Home navigate block intact, selected highlight intact); updated `PlayerBackExitTest` (scrollable actions, clear-before-pop ×2, vertical-stack assertion updated for the scrollable Column). Deliberately NOT touched: player/resolve code, detail-load code, hero, disclaimer, poster, landscape-layout code (parallel builders own them); no backend change; no live-tree / DB touch; no service restart. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict; merge to main; device retest on Samsung RTL (12 credits left) is the only proof for all three issues — CI green proves compile + unit tests, not the tap behaviour on a phone.

**Fixup (CI run 43212637148, commit a6992a8e):** 1435 tests, 1 failed — my own `SearchFilterStateTest` (`a superseded page load never appends its rows`): the assertion counted `if (generation == searchGeneration)` with an unescaped-paren `.toRegex()` — in regex the parens are grouping, so the pattern matched `if generation == searchGeneration` (no parens) and counted 0 against the real single guarded error write in `loadMore`'s catch block. Production code was correct; only the test's regex was wrong. Fix: escaped parens. All five assertions re-verified under Kotlin regex semantics with a mirror script (generation-check counts 4/2/1). Lesson: mirror scripts must use regex semantics, not literal `str.count`, when the test counts via `.toRegex()`. Rebased onto main b9722a4c (Issue 1 sibling merge).

## Session 149 — Player fix v2: gate step-down + gated-preview handling (2026-11-12 ~19:55 PKT)

**What:** completion of the player/cache fix for GOAL 1 (Salaar plays), branch `muse/appfix-player-cache-20261112` from main `c07aac07`, per the brief in `player-cache-fix-brief-20261112.md` (fix directions A–D). **Gate step-down (A):** `qualityGateEnabled` now defaults ON (`ServerSettingsRepository`, `PlayerViewModel`, `DetailViewModel`), so a free viewer is never offered rungs the server will 402; `PlayerPolicyBridge` steps a locked rung down (`playStartPrint`/`stepDownRung`, `playLadder`/`playPrint` stepping down locked rungs). **402 rung failover at start (B):** `isGateRefusalError` / `GATE_REFUSED` (`RenditionFailover.kt`, 402 const), `lowerRungThan` + `switchRungTo` (`AutoQualityDirector`), so a refused rung steps down the staged ladder immediately instead of stalling to the 30 s give-up. **Gated-preview handling (C):** `HlsAheadBuffer` reports a manifest 402 with no redirect as `gated=true` (a 402 after a redirect stays a plain unreachable manifest); `RangeByteFetcher` returns `GateRefused` on the same shape; `AheadFetcher` maps it to `FetchOutcome.GATED`; `CacheCoordinator.handleGated` drops an AUTO_PREVIEW task silently (row + manifest + bytes + file rows deleted, no FAILED chip) and parks a user keep PAUSED with reason `ERR_GATED` (`"gated"`). **Give-up card (D):** "This quality needs VIP" (`PlayerScreen`, `strings.xml`). Scope is GOAL 1 only — detail first-load, Home tab, Series filter, split-deadline and detail-null-fix are NOT in this branch (sequenced follow-ups).

**Checks:** full import diff over all 18 edited Kotlin files vs HEAD (only additions, all used and resolving: `GATE_REFUSED_HTTP_STATUS` ×2, `PlaybackException`/`HttpDataSource`, `QualityOption`, `assertNull`; no removed imports, no dangling issues). Note: the stock `~/workspace/import-diff.py` compares stale local `main` (0a7cc909, 47 commits behind) against HEAD, so it reports pre-existing trunk history and ignores uncommitted work — the worktree-vs-HEAD variant (`/tmp/import-diff-worktree.py`) is the correct run for this branch; its three stock-script flags (PosterCard `Color`, DetailScreen `launch`, SearchScreen `Intent`) are substring false positives (`colorScheme`, fully-qualified `Color.Transparent`, launcher `.launch()`, `hasSearchIntent`). Duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (796 declarations, main + test): no duplicate type name in the same package across files. No `/*` inside KDoc; no new network data class, so no new JSON test needed; brand law untouched. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Fixup 1 (CI run 43243382987, `:app:compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` FAILED):** main compiled and `assembleDebug` succeeded; only the test sources failed — two sibling-test defects, no production change. (1) `SilentFailoverTest` called `isGateRefusalError(null)` but the function takes a non-null `PlaybackException` (its one production caller in `PlaybackService` passes a non-null error) — the null assertion was deleted. (2) `PreResolveTest` called `stepDownRung(ladder, failedBand = "2160p", …)` but the real signature is `stepDownRung(ladder, failed: QualityOption, …)` — all four call sites now pass `failed = rung("2160p", "h2160", true)`; expectations re-traced against the real body (1080p excluded by the 720p cap, provider page and non-playing rungs skipped). Lesson: the coordinator's "diff matches the brief" check verified production code, not that the new tests compile against the real signatures — the test-compile step is the only proof, and it runs only in CI.
