## Session 151 — Salaar play fix: Auto respects VIP + watchdog starts at the URL (2026-11-13 ~05:3x–06:0x PKT)

**What:** the Salaar play fix on branch `app-auto-free-tier` from main `a20a11d4` (restyle tip, already in main), per the live-diagnostics root cause: Auto picked the 2160p 4K print (release 1318138, score 755) which the server's preresolve refuses for a free caller (`premium_quality_requires_vip`, 0 tiers), and the 8 s watchdog killed the play during the 5.7–10.4 s cold resolve of the free 720p print. **Part 1 — Auto respects VIP:** new pure `bestPlayablePrintOf` (`DetailPreResolve.kt`) — the best print the caller may play (playable copy first, any allowed print second; VIP keeps the raw best, free gets the best non-VIP print, null when nothing free remains); `preresolveTopLink` and `playBest` now call it instead of VIP-blind `bestPrintOf` (`playStartPrint` still steps down as defence in depth; `bestPrintOf` itself unchanged, pinned by test). New pure `autoOptionFor` — the VIP-aware Auto rung (server pointer for VIP; for a free caller the gated auto rung falls back to the best free playable rung, same band+label rule as `VipQualityGate.gate`); `warmScopeLink` warms through it, so a free viewer warms the 720p handle, never a gated one. `LadderForScope.autoOption` itself untouched (pinned as the server pointer). **Part 2 — watchdog starts at the URL:** the ViewModel ticker's start clock no longer stamps at Play press — `loadNow` / `retryCurrentCandidates` / `dismissDeadlineGiveUp` record only the request instant (`loadRequestedAtMs`) and arm `startClockPending`; the ticker arms `startAttemptAtMs` on the first connected tick where the player is no longer IDLE (Media3 holds a URL and is loading), with a backdate-to-request fallback so a load that never reaches Media3 still gives up at 30 s. The service stall watchdog `START_WATCHDOG_MS` 8 s → 20 s (the resolve ceiling the diagnostics measured; the 8 s premise — "a cold resolve is 5–11 s, so no frame by 8 s is dead" — is now proven wrong); `ACTIONS_AFTER_MS` 8 s (narration only) and `GIVE_UP_AFTER_MS` 30 s untouched, as are the 6 primaries + 3.0 s auto-hide. Stale "8 s watchdog" comments updated in `DetailPreResolve.kt`, `DetailViewModel.kt`, `PlaybackService.kt`.

**Checks:** full import diff per edited Kotlin file — no import line added or removed in any of the 7 edited files (all new identifiers were already imported or same-package); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (421 files, 790 types): 0 duplicates; new names `bestPlayablePrintOf` (3 files: declaration + 2 call sites + tests) and `autoOptionFor` (1 file) each declared once. New tests: `PreResolveTest` — best playable print free/premium/none/480p-cap + Salaar-shaped 2160p-vs-720p case + scope warm never warms a gated auto rung + null when the gate leaves nothing playable (7 tests); `StartDeadlineTest` — watchdog pinned at 20,000 ms and still below the 30 s give-up, stage(20 s) still ACTIONS. Pinned behaviour kept: `bestPrintOf` raw best, `autoOption` server pointer, 1-arg `scopeWarmHandle` (delegates premium). One Kotlin compile trap caught by hand (no local Gradle by owner order): the ticker fallback reads the mutable `loadRequestedAtMs` into a local val before the null check, since a `private var` cannot be smart-cast. No local Gradle run — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict; merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the VibeView/Samsung retest is the only proof Salaar plays for a free viewer (Auto = 720p, no 402, first frame inside 30 s).** Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, the 402 step-down on the Play-button path (found gap: `playV2Bundle`/`playLink` never stage an AutoLadderPlan, so `lowerRungThan` cannot fire there — follow-up, not this fix), tags/releases.

## Session 152 — Fixup: Auto pill + selection survive the gate, start notice shows elapsed time (2026-11-13 ~06:2x PKT)

**What:** two parent-verified audit findings from `hidden_files/emulator-next-20261113.md`, on branch `app-auto-free-tier` on top of `e20ab738`. **D-1 [P1] — the Auto pill and the Auto selection vanished for a free user:** for Salaar the Quality section showed NO "Auto" pill and NO row highlighted, though Auto is the default and Play presses 720p. Root cause: `VipQualityGate.gate` nulls `autoBand` when the server's auto pointer (2160p) is gated out, and `QualityRungCard` marked isAuto/selected only by `option.band == ladder.autoBand` — with a null pointer nothing matched. Fix: new `LadderForScope.effectiveAutoBand` (`player/QualityLadder.kt`) = `autoOption?.band` — `autoOption` already falls back to the first playable rung, so the model knew the answer and the card now uses it for BOTH the pill and the `selectedBand == AUTO` branch. `VipQualityGate.gate` semantics untouched (other code depends on `autoBand` being null when gated). Copy line also fixed: "Auto plays the best picture this connection can hold." → "Auto plays the best picture your plan can hold." **P-3 [P2] — the 20 s wait was narrated but showed no elapsed time:** new `PlayerUiState.startRequestElapsedMs` (distinct from `startElapsedMs`, which arms only once Media3 holds a URL and would read 0 through the whole resolve), published by the ticker on every tick from the already-tracked `loadRequestedAtMs`, cleared on first frame and zeroed at every reset site (`loadNow`, `retryCurrentCandidates`, `dismissDeadlineGiveUp`) so a new attempt never flashes the last attempt's seconds. New pure `StartDeadline.formatWaitElapsed(rungLabel, elapsedMs)` — null below 10 s, "Trying HD 720p · 12s" with a rung, "12s" without — wired into `StartNoticeCard` as its own line past 10 s (not inside the slow-hint block, because the stage clock starts at the URL and the resolve runs before it). **Give-up copy verified, not changed:** the deadline card reads "This copy did not start within 30 seconds." (30 s give-up, correct); the service failover message renders "start timed out after 20 s with no first frame" (`START_WATCHDOG_MS` = 20_000, derived, not hardcoded); no user-visible string says 8 s anywhere (the only "eight seconds" mentions are KDoc about the actions threshold, which is genuinely 8 s).

**Checks:** full import diff — exactly one added import line (`onAllNodesWithText` in `QualityRungCardTest`), no removed imports; duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (421 files, 796 types): 0 duplicate groups, identical to HEAD (no new top-level types — the additions are a property, a function, and a state field); brace/paren/bracket balance (0,0,0) on all 9 edited files; `formatWaitElapsed` expectations mirror-checked (Kotlin Long division truncates toward zero — the −500 ms case reads null); no `/*` inside added KDoc. New tests (7): `QualityLadderTest` — effective band survives the gate (720p), is the server pointer when ungated (1080p), null on an empty ladder, still the first rung when nothing plays; `StartDeadlineTest` — elapsed line null at 0/9,999/−500 ms, "Trying HD 720p · 10s/12s/20s", bare "12s" with a null/blank rung; `QualityRungCardTest` — exactly one "Auto" pill on a gated ladder (autoBand null, selectedBand AUTO) and on an ungated ladder. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the VibeView/Samsung retest is the only proof the Auto pill sits on 720p and the elapsed line counts.** Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, the 402 step-down staging (assessed only — see checkpoint), tags/releases, the remaining audit findings (D-2, R-1, speed reset, radii, tokens — owed, see `hidden_files/emulator-next-20261113.md`), the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide, 30 s give-up, 8 s actions narration.

## Session 153 — Salaar device-test fixes: gate trusts the live VIP pass, warm carries auth, player waits out the slow resolve, first-tap hardening, 402 step-down on Play (2026-11-13 ~07:1x–08:1x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 1 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 07:06–07:12 PKT, APK app-debug-421, main 95180a6e): Home + all 5 nav tabs passed, but the Auto pill sat on 4K, Play timed out at 30 s with no frame, and the first-tap detail failed. Branch `app-auto-free-tier` from main `95180a6e`, commit `0bbe7035`, 9 files, +228/−17. No push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on Sauce Labs).

**Root causes proven (code + nginx log + live probes, not inferred):** (1) Auto on 4K — the device IS a trial VIP account (anonymous account 201 at 07:06:15, entitlement `vip=true`, vip-pass 200 at 07:06:17, all in the log), so `serverVip=true` → `premiumAccess=true` → `VipQualityGate.gate` returns the ladder unchanged (`VipQualityGate.kt:108`) → `autoBand` stays 2160p. The Session 152 card fix only changed how the card reads the ladder; the ladder itself was never gated for this device. The server disagrees at stream time: the warm (`resolveStream`, no credential) pressed a gated handle and got 402 in 0.014 s, and the Play presses 402'd on gated handles too — while the same 2160p handle pressed with a fresh `?vip=` pass 302s (12.0 s) and with a Bearer key 302s (7.0 s), proven live. So the app believed in a VIP the stream endpoint never saw. (2) Play, no frame — every `/api/v1/stream` press costs 6–12 s of server-side live resolve, and the press is NOT cached (repeat press of the same handle: 8.0 s; warm then press: 6.1 s). Media3's read timeout was 10 s, so it timed out waiting for the 302 before ever touching the file; the five 302s in the log are presses that completed just under (or just after) the timeout. The winner is a 973 MB MKV on googleusercontent (TTFB 2.3 s, non-resumable, Range ignored) — a fast target once reached. (3) First tap — the v2 detail chain is 3 sequential calls; the first tap sent NO API call (log: nothing before 07:08:40; the 07:08:40–44 chain is the Retry), so the failure is app-side before any byte leaves the device (cold connection), and the repository swallows failures to null with only one null retry.

**Fixes:** (1) The gate now trusts the VIP the stream will honor, not the entitlement flag alone: new pure `qualityVipAccess(serverVip, hasLiveVipPass)` (`DetailPreResolve.kt`) — `premiumAccess` is `serverVip && hasLiveVipPass()` (new `SyncAccountRepository.hasLiveVipPass()`, a synchronous in-memory read), so a `vip=true` without a live pass is a free viewer for quality and Auto resolves to 720p; the ladder, card, player Auto and preresolve all read the one `premiumAccess` property. `refreshEntitlement` now warms the pass BEFORE publishing the entitlement, so observers see a consistent state. (2) The warm carries the account: `resolveStream` (Retrofit + `CatalogRepository`) now sends the Bearer key (same pattern as `playV2`); it rides a metadata-only call (`redirect=0`), so no redirect ever carries it onward, and the downloads metadata call compiles unchanged (header last, null default). (3) Player waits out the resolve: `MEDIA_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` 15 s / `MEDIA_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` 25 s (`PlayerDefaults.kt`, used by `PlaybackService`); the 20 s watchdog still bounds each handle and the 30 s give-up still bounds the attempt. (4) First tap: `DETAIL_LOAD_NULL_RETRIES` 1 → 2, plus a once-per-process connection warm-up (`CatalogRepository.warmConnectionIfNeeded`, one cheap stats call, failures swallowed) invoked at the start of `loadDetail`, bounded to 3 s so a slow warm never holds the detail back. (5) 402 step-down on the Play path (assessed in Session 152, now built): `playV2Bundle` stages the gated ladder's rungs for the winner's band (`rungForBand(ladder, print.resolution.wire)` → `stageAutoLadder`) before `playLink`, and `stageAutoLadder` now computes `auto` from `ladder.effectiveAutoBand` (new pure `isAutoRung`) instead of the gate-nulled `autoBand`, so a gated Auto play is still recognised as Auto and a 402 steps down instead of giving up.

**Checks:** full import diff — exactly two added imports (`AtomicBoolean` in `CatalogRepository`, `withTimeoutOrNull` in `DetailViewModel`), zero removed; duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (421 files, 791 types): 0 duplicate groups, no new top-level types; brace/paren/bracket balance (0,0,0) on all 9 edited files; no `/*` inside added KDoc (the 8 matches are KDoc openers). New tests (6): `PreResolveTest` — `qualityVipAccess` truth table, gated Auto rung still Auto when `autoBand` is null, ungated Auto rung is the server pointer, `rungForBand` finds the winner band and nothing else, null detail gets two retries; `PlayerDefaultsTest` — read timeout clears the measured 12 s resolve ceiling and connect < read. All six expectations mirror-checked in Python (Kotlin Long division / first-match semantics). No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the Sauce Labs S2 re-test is the only proof Salaar plays (Auto = 720p, first frame inside 30 s, first tap loads).** Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the remaining audit findings (D-2, R-1, speed reset, radii, tokens), the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide, 30 s give-up, 8 s actions narration. Known follow-up (not this fix): the downloads metadata resolve (`DeviceDownloadsRepository.kt:196`) still calls `resolveStream` anonymously, so a VIP downloading a 4K print is refused at the metadata step before the download URL (which does carry the pass) is built — same class as the warm bug, owed its own fix.

## Session 155 — Sauce Labs S2 fixes: no same-rendition retry before the first frame, start error reported, detail pre-network failure retried (2026-11-13 ~09:1x–10:0x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 2 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 09:00–09:12 PKT, APK app-debug-425, main 7e12675e): Auto on HD (720p) now PASSES (the Session 153 VIP-pass gate works — do not break it), but the first-tap detail STILL fails and Play STILL times out at 30 s with no frame. Branch `app-s2-fixes` from main `7e12675e`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on Sauce Labs).

**Root causes proven (nginx log + a live file-host probe, not inferred):** (1) Play, no frame — during the 3 Play attempts the app pressed `/api/v1/stream` 31 times and got 22 × 302 (playable link served) + 9 × 200 (metadata); the server serves playable links, the player renders no frame. A live probe of the winner (post 79881, first handle) proves the file IS a valid MKV (EBML header, `video/mkv`, 973,535,538 bytes) BUT the host IGNORES Range: a ranged GET `bytes=0-1023` returned status 200 + the ENTIRE 973 MB file (no Accept-Ranges, no Content-Range). So Media3 cannot seek this file, and the player's failover machine retries the SAME rendition (`retrySame=1`) on failure — each retry re-presses the redirector (6–12 s of live server resolve) — so the 30 s start budget is spent re-resolving one unplayable copy instead of trying a different file. (2) First-tap detail — the first tap sends NO API call (log: no detail or warm call at 09:03/09:05; the warm stats call appears only at 09:04:17, one second before the 3rd tap's detail chain, proving `connectionWarmed` was still false and `loadDetail()` never ran on the first two taps). When the tap DOES reach the Detail screen, `loadDetail()` throws before any network call (the error "Failed to load details. Check connection." comes only from the `catch (ex: Exception)` branch), and the Retry succeeds — so the failure is a first-call, pre-network readiness failure in the device-side setup, not a server refusal. The only pre-network throw the code names with certainty is `ApiProvider.current()` throwing `IllegalStateException` ("Connect to a catalog server first").

**Fixes:** (1) A start failure never retries the same rendition: `FailoverStateMachine.onFailure` takes `beforeFirstFrame` (default false); when true it skips the `RetrySame` branch and goes straight to a backup handle / the next rank. `PlaybackService.onPlayerError` passes `beforeFirstFrame = !StartDeadline.hasFirstFrame(player.playbackState)`. After the first frame the policy's `retrySame` still applies. (2) The exact Media3 start error is now reported, not just logged to logcat (which the device test never returns): new `PlayerSessionStore.lastStartError` (record/clear); the service records the `summarize(error)` on a start failure and clears it on STATE_READY and on every new load/retry; `PlayerViewModel.reportLinkFailedOnce` sends it as the `failed` report's `error` (fallback "Playback did not start"), so the server names the cause. (3) Detail pre-network failure is retried instead of hard-failed: `CatalogRepository.getWorkDetailV2` and `getPostDetail` now catch `IllegalStateException` from the provider and return null, so the existing null-retry (2 null retries, already tested) re-asks instead of the screen hard-failing on the first tap; `loadDetail()`'s warm moved inside the try block (defense in depth). `isTransientDetailError` untouched (the existing test pins IllegalStateException as non-transient for the exception path — the provider failure now travels the null path instead).

**Checks:** full import diff — no import line added or removed in any of the 6 edited files (all new identifiers already imported or same-package); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (types only): the 2 groups found are pre-existing same-file matches in files I did not touch (PlayerGestures.kt `None`, PhoneCredentialGuardTest.kt `SyncContract`); my 2 new test classes each declared once. No `/*` inside added KDoc. New tests (10): `RenditionFailoverStartTest` (5) — start failure hops to backup at once, advances to next rank with no backup, hands off on the last rank, still retries same after the first frame, start failure consumes the retry so a later failure hops on; `PlayerSessionStoreStartErrorTest` (5) — null at rest, record reads back, clear returns null, blank/null records nothing, new error replaces the old. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the Sauce Labs S3 re-test is the only proof the first tap loads, Play reaches a frame, and the `failed` report carries the real Media3 error.** Known gap (stated plainly): the tap-to-navigation half of the first-tap failure (the tap not reaching `loadDetail()` at all) was NOT isolated to a single UI defect from static analysis — the PosterCard `combinedClickable` + `pressScale`/`PressHaptics` and the `navigateToPost` route both read correct, and the S2 evidence is equally consistent with the first `loadDetail()` throwing pre-network (which this fix addresses) as with the tap being dropped; if S3 still shows a dead first tap, the next step is a tap-level trace, not another retry. Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the 402 step-down (kept), the Auto VIP-pass gate (kept — Auto stays on 720p), the 20 s watchdog, the 30 s give-up, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

## Session 156 — Sauce Labs S3 fixes: Matroska tail-cue seek disabled, detail retry treats provider-not-ready as transient (2026-11-13 ~12:2x–12:47 PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 3 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 11:56–12:12 PKT, APK app-debug-427, main 4ed97eb0): Salaar detail first tap now PASSES, Tarzan's Revenge PLAYS, but Salaar Play STILL shows no frame and Tarzan's detail first tap STILL fails. Branch `app-s3-fixes` from main `4ed97eb0`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on Sauce Labs S4).

**Root causes proven (nginx log + live probes + a real Media3 extractor run, not inferred):** (1) Salaar Play, no frame — the log shows the app pressed `/api/v1/stream` 13 times and the server answered 302 eight times, yet the player never showed a frame, and the same link token was reported failed twice. A live probe of the v2 play bundle for work 74537 (release 1318132, guest, Auto 720p) shows only 2 stream candidates, BOTH `accepts_ranges: false`, `resumable: false`, kind `cdn_direct` — and a probe of every other playable Salaar print finds the same: every candidate ignores Range, several prints have 0 candidates, 1080p prints are 402-gated for a free account. There is NO Range-supporting playable Salaar link at any band, so the Session 155 hop has nowhere to go. The winner file is a 973,535,486-byte MKV; reading its head shows mkvmerge v4.9.1 put the Cues element at the tail (offset 973,456,222, from the SeekHead). Driving the real Media3 1.3.1 MatroskaExtractor over a synthetic file in exactly that shape (380-byte prefix, SeekHead advertising Cues at 973,456,222, input reporting the real 973,535,486-byte length) proves the mechanism: unflagged, the extractor seeks to 973,456,274 and emits no sample — on a Range-ignoring host that seek is skip-by-reading ~973 MB, so the 20 s watchdog / 30 s give-up fires first; with `FLAG_DISABLE_SEEK_FOR_CUES` it seeks nowhere and emits a sample, and the factory path (`DefaultExtractorsFactory.setMatroskaExtractorFlags`) passes the flag through identically. (2) Tarzan detail first tap — the log shows NO `/api/v1/posts/157747` call before the Retry's call at 12:02:11 (200), so the first tap died before any network call, and the screen showed the exception text ("Failed to load details. Check connection."), which is exactly the `friendlyNetworkError` fallback for an `IllegalStateException` — no transport error produces that text. The repository already catches that throw on both detail paths (Session 155), so the remaining hole is the retry policy: `isTransientDetailError` classed `IllegalStateException` as non-transient, so any escape hard-fails the first tap at once instead of retrying, and three attempts over ~2.1 s proved too short for a real connection blip.

**Fixes:** (1) The player no longer seeks for Matroska cues: new `PlayerExtractors.kt` builds the extractors factory with `FLAG_DISABLE_SEEK_FOR_CUES` (`playerMatroskaExtractorFlags`, also the value the tests drive), and `PlaybackService` hands that factory to its `DefaultMediaSourceFactory` at player build time. Trade-off, documented in code: a tail-cued MKV becomes unseekable (the extractor says so in its seek map); well-muxed MKVs and all non-MKV containers are unaffected. The scrub-preview player (`ScrubPreview.kt`) is deliberately untouched — its seeks are its purpose, and its failures are silent by contract; if tail-cued files also stall previews, that is a separate, unobserved surface. (2) Detail retry: `isTransientDetailError` now treats `IllegalStateException` as transient, with an explicit `CancellationException` guard first (cancellation extends `IllegalStateException` and must still abort at once — the retry loop already rethrows it separately, and the guard keeps the policy correct even if that order ever changes); `DETAIL_LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` goes 3 → 4 (one more 1.5 s backoff, only spent when all three before it failed). No timeout was raised as a substitute for a fix: the 20 s watchdog and 30 s give-up are untouched, as is the Session 153 VIP-pass gate (Auto stays 720p, never 4K for a free/VIP-trial account).

**Checks:** no local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. The extractor fix was proven before the app change by a local JVM harness driving the real Media3 1.3.1 jars (`/tmp/mkvharness/`, synthetic tail-cued MKV + fake 973 MB input): unflagged seeks `[973456274]`, 0 samples; flagged seeks `[]`, 1 sample, unseekable; factory with flag identical to direct flagged, factory without identical to direct unflagged. Full import diff per edited Kotlin file (script compares main...HEAD, run after commit); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (types only) — new names (`buildPlayerExtractorsFactory`, `playerMatroskaExtractorFlags`, `PlayerExtractorsTest`, `DetailRetryPolicyTest`) each declared once. No `/*` inside added KDoc (the one `/*` in the diff is pre-existing in `PlaybackService.kt:154`). New tests (12): `PlayerExtractorsTest` (5, Robolectric) — unflagged extractor seeks to the tail cues, flagged extractor starts from the first cluster, player factory serves MKV through a Matroska extractor, extractor from the player factory does not seek for cues, extractor from an unflagged factory seeks for cues (the control that keeps the flagged tests honest); `DetailRetryPolicyTest` (7, plain JVM) — provider not ready retries, transport failures retry, retryable HTTP statuses retry, deterministic statuses fail fast, anything else fails fast, cancellation is never transient, retry budget is four attempts. Updated `PreResolveTest` (2 assertions): the old test pinned `IllegalStateException` as non-transient and the budget at 3 — both now assert the new policy, with a Session 156 note. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the Sauce Labs S4 re-test is the only proof Salaar reaches a frame and the first tap loads.** Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the 402 step-down (kept), the Auto VIP-pass gate (kept), the 20 s watchdog, the 30 s give-up, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

**Parent fixup (same session, before push):** hand-verifying the new tests against the implementation caught one real bug — `DetailRetryPolicyTest` asserts `java.util.concurrent.CancellationException` is never transient, but the `isTransientDetailError` guard only excluded `kotlinx.coroutines.CancellationException`. The java.util one is a sibling, not a subclass, of the coroutines one (both extend `IllegalStateException`), so it would have fallen through to the transient branch and a cancelled load would have retried instead of aborting — the test would have failed in CI. Fix: the guard now excludes both cancellation types. The parent also re-ran the `/tmp/mkvharness/` Media3 harness independently and reproduced the extractor proof exactly (unflagged seeks `[973456274]`, 0 samples; flagged seeks `[]`, 1 sample, unseekable; factory paths identical).

**Self-check lint fixups (same session, before push):** Android CI 43389390510 failed on `:app:lintDebug` with 4 × `UnsafeOptInUsageError` — `MatroskaExtractor.FLAG_DISABLE_SEEK_FOR_CUES` is `@UnstableApi` and the new property used it unannotated. Fixup 2 (f5d93de5) annotated `playerMatroskaExtractorFlags` with `@OptIn(UnstableApi::class)` (the established `androidx.annotation.OptIn` form). CI 43390644946 then failed on lint again with 3 errors, all in `buildPlayerExtractorsFactory` (PlayerExtractors.kt:36–38): the return type `DefaultExtractorsFactory`, its constructor, and `.setMatroskaExtractorFlags(...)` are each `@UnstableApi`, and the property annotation does not cover usages inside the factory function. Fixup 3 annotates `buildPlayerExtractorsFactory` itself with `@OptIn(UnstableApi::class)` — one annotation covers all three usages. Assemble + unit tests were green in both runs (only lint failed); the debug APK artifact still uploaded on the failed run.
## Session 157 — Sauce Labs S4 fixes: provider failure on the detail path answers null, double-tap cannot stack details, playback forces landscape (2026-11-13 ~14:0x–14:2x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 4 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 13:38–13:48 PKT, APK app-debug-432, main e606b48e): Salaar PLAYS with a real picture (GOAL 1 met for Salaar) and Salaar's detail first tap PASSES, but Tarzan's Revenge detail first tap STILL fails and the video renders sideways in portrait on both titles. Branch `app-s4-fixes` from main `e606b48e`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on Sauce Labs S5).

**Root causes proven (nginx log + code reading, not inferred):** (1) Tarzan first tap — the log proves the tap made NO network call: `works/132312` appears exactly once in the whole access log (13:44:59, the Retry's call) and `posts/157747` once (13:45:01, the bridge of that same load); there is not one device line between the 13:44:29 search and 13:44:59, and only 9 device lines in the whole 13:44 minute. The screen showed "Couldn't load this title / Failed to load details. Check connection." — exactly `loadDetail`'s catch fallback, and only that branch can print it (the guard prints "This title could not be opened.", the null path prints "This title is not available on the connected server.", cancellation is rethrown and paints no error at all). The Search screen's own error title is "Couldn't search", so the screen the tester saw WAS the Detail screen — `loadDetail` RAN and threw before any byte left the device (this is not the S2 pattern, where `loadDetail` never ran at all). Eliminated by reading every step of that path: the warm cannot throw (`runCatching` inside, bounded to 3 s); `detailWarmAudio` / `RegionChoices.byId` / `UserRegion.normalize` are null-safe and pure; the `serverUrl` flow yields a non-blank URL or null (null becomes the provider `IllegalStateException`, which the repository already catches to null — the wrong text for this screen); `getWorkDetailV2` catches `HttpException` / `IOException` / `IllegalStateException` to null. The one pre-network failure class the repository did NOT catch is a non-`IllegalStateException` `RuntimeException` from the provider or client-build step (a bad address, a client build that throws): it escapes past the catches, the retry policy classes it non-transient, and Session 156's four attempts never run — the tap hard-fails at once with the fallback error, while the manual Retry (seconds later, provider healthy again) loads fine. The exact transient member of that class that threw in S4 is NOT identified from code plus log — no pre-network thrower is visible for a valid URL, and the provider was healthy seconds before (search 200) and after (Retry 200) — so the fix covers the whole provider-failure class instead of naming one member. (2) Sideways video — the player ALREADY requests landscape on entry (`SCREEN_ORIENTATION_SENSOR_LANDSCAPE`, `PlayerScreenContent` entry effect) and the manifest pins no orientation, so the sideways picture is most plausibly the Sauce stream not rotating its frame (the app rotated, the viewer did not), not a missing request. The genuine app-side gap is narrower: sensor landscape depends on the orientation sensor, which is fixed, flat or slow on a cloud device, so the request can hold the player in portrait. The fix forces landscape.

**Fixes:** (1) The detail path's provider failures now answer null: `CatalogRepository.getWorkDetailV2` and `getPostDetail` both catch `RuntimeException` from the `apiProvider.current()` call and return null — the same failure-to-ask shape Session 155 built for the not-ready error — so the caller's null-retry re-asks instead of the screen hard-failing on the first tap. Both catches rethrow `CancellationException` first, in both its forms (coroutines and `java.util.concurrent`, which are siblings, not subclasses — Session 156 parent fixup), so a cancelled load still aborts at once instead of answering null and retrying. A dead socket still throws (`IOException` is not a `RuntimeException`), so the caller can still tell a refusal from a failure to ask; a mapping failure still throws after the HTTP call, so it stays a hard error, as it should. (2) `ApiProvider` is `open` with `current()` open, so a test can stand in a provider that throws without a server. (3) `navigateToPost` now navigates with `launchSingleTop = true` — a double-tap on a poster cannot stack two identical detail entries (same rule as the player route, Session 114); a different title is a different route, so related titles still open. (4) Playback forces landscape: the entry effect and the orientation-state mapping both request `SCREEN_ORIENTATION_LANDSCAPE` (the portrait branch of the rotate toggle keeps the sensor, so an explicit flip to portrait still follows how the viewer holds the device); the dispose step still restores `UNSPECIFIED` on exit.

**Checks:** full import diff per edited Kotlin file (script compares main...HEAD, run after commit); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (types only) — new names (`CatalogDetailProviderFailureTest`, `ThrowingApiProvider`) each declared once; brace/paren/bracket balance on all edited files; no `/*` inside added KDoc. New tests (5): `CatalogDetailProviderFailureTest` (Robolectric — the repository needs a real `ServerSettingsRepository`, which needs an application context; the provider is a fake subclass whose `current()` throws, so no test touches the network) — v2 detail answers null on a bad-address provider failure, v1 detail answers null on the same, v2 detail rethrows a coroutines cancellation instead of answering null, v2 detail rethrows the `java.util` cancellation instead of answering null, v2 detail still answers null for the provider-not-ready error. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the Sauce Labs S5 re-test is the only proof the first tap loads and playback is landscape.** S5 procedure note: rotate the Sauce device to landscape BEFORE the play step — the S4 sideways picture is most plausibly the stream frame not rotating, so a portrait device frame will still show the video sideways even with this fix, and that would be a false failure; if S5 still shows sideways with the device already landscape, it is the app. Known limit, stated plainly: the fix converts a provider failure into the null-retry path (three asks over ~2.1 s), it does not lengthen that window — if the provider outage in S4 outlasted ~2.1 s, the screen would have shown "not available" instead of the fallback and the manual Retry would still have been needed; the outage duration is not knowable from the log (tap time is minute-granular), so lengthening the window would be guessing. The S2 tap-drop hazard (first taps on which `loadDetail` never ran at all) is NOT fixed by this change: if S5 shows a first tap that stays on Search with no error screen and no navigation, that is the tap-drop, not this fix — the next step for it is a tap-level trace, and the search submit re-firing an identical debounced search (a recomposition window under the tap) is the prime suspect. Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, `isTransientDetailError` (mapping errors stay non-transient — correct), `DETAIL_LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (4, unchanged), the connection warm, the Session 153 VIP-pass gate (Auto stays 720p, never 4K for a free/VIP-trial account), the 20 s watchdog, the 30 s give-up, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

## Session 158 — Sauce Labs S5 fixes: detail catch widened to any failure to ask, identical search re-submit deduped, dead handles never re-pressed, player exits to the device's orientation (2026-11-13 ~15:0x–15:4x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 5 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 14:38–14:49 PKT, APK app-debug-434, main e7c217b2): Salaar detail first tap FAILS (tap-drop, no navigation, no error), Tarzan detail first tap STILL FAILS (navigated, "Couldn't load this title / Failed to load details. Check connection."), Tarzan Play FAILS (server served a 302, player showed "Finding your video" + "Cache engine failed" toast, then "Nothing playable here right now", 0:00, black screen, 0 progress heartbeats), the player then hammered `/api/v1/stream` ~1/sec (239x404 + 136x429, session 429'd), and after exiting the Salaar player the detail stayed landscape while the device frame was portrait. Sauce Labs trial minutes are EXHAUSTED (~54m of 60) — there is no S6 on Sauce; verification is CI only, and the next device test (BrowserStack / user phone / Robo) is the parent's to arrange. Branch `app-s5-fixes` from main `e7c217b2`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on a device).

**Root causes proven (nginx log + line-by-line code reading, not inferred):** (1) Salaar tap-drop — the log shows ONE search at 14:39:55 and nothing until the second tap's `works/114578` at 14:40:54, and the code shows why a first tap lands in a storm: typing fires a debounced search and pressing Search (IME) fires an immediate one for the SAME query, so the S5 log shows "Tarzan" submitted twice 2 s apart (14:46:20 + 14:46:22) and "Tarzan's Revenge" twice the same way; the second fetch cancels the first job, re-fetches, and re-emits the whole list (fresh Post instances, regrouped) plus the suggestions landing and the recents echo, all in the window the viewer taps a result in. The first tap drops; the second (after everything settles) always works. The exact Compose-internal drop mechanism is NOT provable from static reading (stable row keys + same list reference should skip item recomposition on the suggestions/recents updates), so the fix removes the log-proven storm and hardens the keys rather than claiming a single internal cause. (2) Tarzan first tap — re-verified every link of the pre-network path in this clone: `warmConnectionIfNeeded` cannot throw (`runCatching` inside, bounded to 3 s); `detailWarmAudio(userRegion)` is pure (`RegionChoices.byId` falls back to default, `UserRegion.normalize` is a pure fold); `ApiProvider.current()` / `forServer()` throw only `IllegalStateException` / `IllegalArgumentException` / `IOException` / `HttpException` (all already caught to null by Session 157); `normalizeServerUrl` converts its `URI` throw to `IllegalArgumentException`; `toPostDetail` runs AFTER the network call, so the zero-works-calls log fact excludes it. **The exact Tarzan thrower is NOT identifiable from code alone** — the remaining class is a CHECKED exception the catches do not name (a `TimeoutException`, a `CertificateException` from the TLS stack, any other checked throw from a library on this path). (3) Player hammering — the player hands Media3 the REDIRECTOR URL as its media item (`startStreamHandle` builds `streamRedirectUrl(...)`), and there is NO custom `LoadErrorHandlingPolicy` anywhere, so Media3's default retries the redirector on 404/429; the log proves it with the `RaddStream/0.1.0 (Android Media3)` user-agent pressing alongside the app's own okhttp pairs every 3 s (failover hops + cache-engine re-resolve/retry, each bounded on its own — 6 engine attempts, bounded failover ranks — but unbounded in combination, and each 429 is itself a load error that triggers more retries). The 30/min/IP bucket trips, then everything 429s. The failover machine also had no "dead handle" concept: after the first frame, `RetrySame` re-pressed the same 404'd address. (4) Orientation — the player exit dispose restores `SCREEN_ORIENTATION_UNSPECIFIED`, which does NOT snap the app back on a device whose rotation is locked (a cloud device, or a phone with auto-rotate off) — the detail stays sideways until the device is turned, exactly the S5 observation. (5) Tarzan Play, no frame — the 302 at 14:47:26 was served to an okhttp press (the cache engine's fetch, which follows the redirect), so the file fetch after the 302 failed in BOTH Media3 and the engine (the engine's session went FAILED, which is the "Cache engine failed" toast: `CacheAffordance.uiStateFor` maps `CacheState.FAILED` to it). Which link the 302 served cannot be decoded from the nginx log — the log carries no query strings, so the handle is not in it; the parent decodes it with `redact.parse_token`. The file-fetch failure itself is a backend/data finding (the file host refused or died after the 302), NOT an app-code change — the app-side fix is stopping the hammering that followed it.

**Fixes:** (1) The detail path now answers null on ANY failure to ask: `CatalogRepository.getWorkDetailV2` and `getPostDetail` both catch `Exception` after the existing catches (both `CancellationException` forms still rethrown first) and return null, so the caller's null-retry re-asks; the swallowed exception's class + message is logged (`Log.w("CatalogRepository", "detail ask failed without a request: ...")`) so the next device run names the thrower in logcat instead of failing silently. The v1 path rethrows `IOException` out of that catch — a dead socket still propagates there by design (Session 155), so the caller can tell a refusal from a failure to ask; the v2 path already catches `IOException` to null above it. (2) An identical search whose results are already on screen is now a no-op: new pure `shouldSkipIdenticalSearch` + `CompletedSearchKey` (`SearchViewModel.kt`), checked at the top of `scheduleSearch` BEFORE any job is cancelled, page reset, or loading flashed; recorded on both success paths (v2 + v1), cleared on error. A new query, filter, or sort, an empty list, or an error on screen all still search; pagination (`loadMore`, its own job + generation) is untouched. Trade-off, stated: re-submitting the identical search in the same screen session no longer refreshes; leaving the screen and returning re-fetches. (3) Search result rows are now keyed by content (`"${group.typeKey}-${pair.joinToString(",") { it.id }}"`) instead of row index, so a re-emitted list cannot shift rows under a press in flight. (4) A dead handle never retries the same address: new `isDeadHandleError` (`RenditionFailover.kt`, same two-signal pattern as `isGateRefusalError` — message "Response code: 404" or the cause-chain `InvalidResponseCodeException`, for 404/403/410), passed from `PlaybackService.onPlayerError` into `FailoverStateMachine.onFailure` as `isDeadHandle`, which gates the `RetrySame` branch (default false, so every existing caller is unchanged). The 402 stays out of the dead set — a gate refusal steps down, it does not hop. (5) Media3 never retries a final answer from the redirector: new `RedirectorLoadErrorPolicy` (`player/`, wraps `DefaultLoadErrorHandlingPolicy`) returns `C.TIME_UNSET` for 402/403/404/410/429 and delegates everything else, installed on the `DefaultMediaSourceFactory` in `PlaybackService` at player build time. (6) Player exit now snaps to the device's actual orientation: the dispose sets `SCREEN_ORIENTATION_SENSOR` (follow the physical sensor now), then posts a 1 s delayed restore to `UNSPECIFIED` on the decor view, so the rest of the app is not sensor-driven forever; the delayed post survives the composition and is a no-op on a finishing activity.

**Checks:** no local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. Full import diff — exactly two added imports in tracked files (`android.util.Log` in `CatalogRepository`, `java.io.IOException` in the provider-failure test), zero removed; every import in the three new files is used. Duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree — no new duplicate groups (the 8 groups the scan reports are pre-existing same-file/crude-regex matches in files untouched here, including the two `PlayerScreen.kt` self-matches; no Session 158 name appears in any group). Brace/paren/bracket balance (0,0,0) on all 8 edited + 3 new files. No `/*` inside added KDoc (the 6 matches are KDoc openers). New tests (22): `CatalogDetailProviderFailureTest` +4 (Robolectric — v2/v1 detail answer null on a checked `TimeoutException` from the provider, v1 still propagates a dead socket, v2 still answers null for one); `SearchResubmitDedupTest` +7 (plain JVM — identical search with results + no error skips; different query / filter / sort / no results / error / no completed search all still search); `RenditionFailoverStartTest` +3 (plain JVM — dead handle after the first frame hops to the backup, advances to the next rank with no backup, hands off on the last rank); `SilentFailoverTest` +3 (detector — 404/403/410 recognised, 402 + timeout rejected, pinning the gate-refusal/dead-handle split); `RedirectorLoadErrorPolicyTest` +5 (plain JVM — 404/410/403/402/429 never retry, 500/503/no-status defer to the default). Every new test was hand-checked against the implementation it pins (the Session 156 parent-fixup lesson: a test that asserts a behaviour the code does not have is a CI failure, not a test). **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the next device test (BrowserStack / user phone / Robo) is the only proof the first tap loads, the player stops hammering, and the exit orientation snaps back.** Deliberately NOT touched: backend/Fast_Fetch code (the Tarzan 302's target link is a backend/data finding — decode the handle from the log, do not change app code for it), live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the cache engine's own retry policy (already bounded: 6 attempts with backoff, `MAX_RERESOLVES = 2` — the hammering was the product of three systems, and the two unbounded-ish ones are now capped), `isTransientDetailError`, `DETAIL_LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (4, unchanged), the connection warm, the Session 153 VIP-pass gate (Auto stays 720p, never 4K for a free/VIP-trial account), the 20 s watchdog, the 30 s give-up, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

**Self-check fixup (2026-11-13 ~15:3x PKT, after push):** branch CI run 43408632674 FAILED on `:app:compileDebugKotlin` — two compile errors the verification missed: (1) `RedirectorLoadErrorPolicy` imported `DefaultLoadErrorHandlingPolicy` / `LoadErrorHandlingPolicy` from `androidx.media3.exoplayer.source` (wrong package — in Media3 1.3.1 both live in `androidx.media3.exoplayer.upstream`, as the `PlaybackService` expected-type error also showed); imports corrected to `.upstream`. (2) `SearchScreen.kt:618` `joinToString { it.id }` — `Post.id` is `Int`, the transform must return `CharSequence`; now `it.id.toString()`. No other `joinToString { it.id }` in the tree. Fixup committed + pushed by self-check; CI re-run is the verification (parent merges).

**Self-check fixup 2 (2026-11-13 ~15:4x PKT, after push):** fixup CI run 43409810019 FAILED again on `:app:compileDebugKotlin` — Media3 1.3.1's `LoadErrorHandlingPolicy` has no `getRetryDelayMs`: the retry method is `getRetryDelayMsFor`, and the interface also demands `getFallbackSelectionFor` (verified against the Android developer API reference for `DefaultLoadErrorHandlingPolicy`). Renamed the override to `getRetryDelayMsFor` and added `getFallbackSelectionFor` — terminal redirector statuses return null (no fallback to another location/track of the same redirector URL; the failover machine owns the next move), everything else delegates to the default. The unit tests pin only the pure `redirectorRetryDelayMs` table, so no test change was needed. Fixup committed + pushed by self-check; CI re-run is the verification (parent merges).

**Parent fixup 3 (2026-11-13 ~15:5x PKT, after push):** fixup-2 CI run 43410612829 FAILED on `:app:lintDebug` — `RedirectorLoadErrorPolicy.kt:41: UnsafeOptInUsageError`: implementing Media3's `LoadErrorHandlingPolicy` (an `@UnstableApi` interface) needs `@OptIn(UnstableApi::class)` on the class, the same pattern `PlaybackService` / `PlayerExtractors` / `PlaybackMediaItemFactory` already use. Added the import + annotation (2 lines). The test file needs nothing — it pins only the pure `redirectorRetryDelayMs` table and never instantiates the policy. Fixup committed + pushed by parent; CI re-run is the verification.

**Self-check fixup 4 (2026-11-13 ~15:5x PKT, after push):** fixup-3 CI run 43411818315 FAILED again on `:app:lintDebug` — compile + unit tests PASSED, lint still failed with the same `UnsafeOptInUsageError` at `RedirectorLoadErrorPolicy.kt:43` (all 3 `Error:` lines in the log point at the class declaration; 15 lint errors counted are repeats of this one issue). Root cause: in Media3 1.3.1 `UnstableApi` is NOT a `@RequiresOptIn` marker, so `@OptIn(UnstableApi::class)` is ignored by kotlinc (warning at log line 1311: "not an opt-in requirement marker; therefore, its usage in @OptIn is ignored") and does not satisfy lint for a class that IMPLEMENTS an unstable interface — lint requires the class itself to carry `@UnstableApi` so the instability propagates to its users (that is what the lint message prescribes: "marked with @androidx.media3.common.util.UnstableApi"). Fix: the class now carries `@UnstableApi` directly, with a comment recording why `@OptIn` cannot work here. The `UnstableApi` import is unchanged. No test change — the test file pins only the pure table and never instantiates the policy; `PlaybackService` instantiates the policy from its own `@OptIn(UnstableApi::class)` context, which lint accepts for a usage. Fixup committed + pushed by self-check; CI re-run is the verification (parent merges).


## Session 154 — Design audit P2/P3: reduced motion, radii, nav tokens, dead motion tokens, type scale (2026-11-13 ~07:3x PKT)

**What:** the remaining audit findings from `hidden_files/emulator-next-20261113.md` (the P1 D-1 Auto pill is Session 153's, in a different worktree), on branch `app-design-p2p3-20261113` from main `95180a6e`. **D-2 [P2] — QualityRungCard rung morph ignored Reduce Motion:** the selected rung's corner morph (12→28dp) rode `RaddMotion.defaultSpatial()` (0.8/380 spring, overshoots) with no reduced-motion check. Fix: `rememberReduceMotion()` in `RungRow`; under reduced motion the corner snaps (`snap()`, instant) instead of springing. **R-1 [P2] — RegionSelectionScreen hardcoded tweens + no reduced motion:** the card stagger used raw `tween(300)` and the check badge used raw `tween(200)` in / `tween(150)` out (150ms is below every token). Fix: stagger now rides `RaddMotion.DurationDefault` (250ms) with the index * 90 stagger kept, and reduced-motion users get the fade only, never the rise (`EnterTransition.None` — the HomeScreen rails pattern); the badge rides `RaddMotion.default()` in (250ms) / `RaddMotion.quick()` out (120ms), and under reduced motion it is a 30ms alpha crossfade (`FadeWhenReduced`), never a scale. **N-1 [P3] — two off-scale 24dp radii:** `HeroCarousel.kt` indicator pill clip and `NavGraph.kt` bottom-nav indicator shape were `RoundedCornerShape(24.dp)` (M3 scale has no 24: 20 → 28). Fix: both now use `RaddTokens.Shape.Pill` (fully round, 999dp). **N-2 [P3] — bottom-nav 64×48dp pill / 80dp tab vs M3 56×32dp / 64dp:** deliberately RECORDED as the app's own tokens, not shrunk — Robo runs 4–6 measured the 32dp pill under the 48dp touch-target floor, and the 80dp content height is what stops the system nav-bars inset from crushing the tabs to 32dp (run 6). New `RaddTokens.Touch.NavIndicatorWidth` (64dp), `NavIndicatorHeight` (48dp), `NavTabHeight` (80dp), used by `BottomNavTab`; the reasoning is pinned in the token KDoc and the existing `BottomNavTouchTargetTest` still pins the rendered bar. **T-1 [P3] — dead duplicate motion tokens:** `RaddTokens.Motion` held `FastMs/NormalMs/SlowMs/EmphasisMs/ShimmerMs/FeedbackMs`, `EaseOut/EaseStandard/EaseIn`, and six `Spring*Damping/Stiffness` constants — all unused (grep-verified 0 usages outside the token file) and their values did not match the skill; the live system is `RaddMotion` (Theme.kt). Fix: deleted all of them; only `PressScale` (used by `pressScale`) survives, and the now-unused `CubicBezierEasing` import is gone. **T-2 [P3] — DurationSlow 600ms > ~500ms cap:** `RaddMotion.DurationSlow` 600 → 500ms (currently unused — 0 call sites — so the cap is token hygiene, not a behaviour change). **brandSp literals:** all 19 off-scale literals in `PlayerScreen.kt` are now on the M3 scale — 17× `brandSp(13)` → `brandSp(14)` (all body copy: banner, snackbar, up-next, failure/offline/resume/calibration bodies, speed preset, sleep timer, subtitle settings, audio boost, debug row), 1× `brandSp(10)` → `brandSp(11)` (the control-button short label — Label Small, smallest on-scale size; the E-C3 comment now notes the change), 1× `brandSp(15)` → `brandSp(16)` (`SheetTitle` — Title Medium). The computed `brandSp(16f * sizePercent / 100f)` subtitle size is untouched. **P-1 [P2] — playback speed carries across titles:** ASSESSED ONLY, not implemented — it touches `PlayerViewModel`, which Session 153 owns right now. The exact change is in the checkpoint (`hidden_files/app-design-p2p3-20261113.md`): reset speed to 1.0f in `loadNow` (`PlayerViewModel.kt:1257`); do NOT reset in `retryCurrentCandidates` (:1538) or `dismissDeadlineGiveUp` (:1583) — both are same-title retries where the viewer's chosen speed must stay; reset-only (no DataStore) is the recommendation — it is the streaming-UX standard, a 2-line change, and the speed sheet already shows the current speed so the reset is visible.

**Checks:** full import diff — added: `snap` (QualityRungCard), `EnterTransition` + `rememberReduceMotion` + `RaddMotion` (RegionSelectionScreen), `RaddTokens` (HeroCarousel, NavGraph), `assertTrue` (RaddTokensTest); removed: `RoundedCornerShape` (NavGraph — its only use was the 24dp indicator), `CubicBezierEasing` (RaddTokens — its only users were the deleted Ease* vals); duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree (581 type declarations): 0 duplicate groups, no new top-level types (the additions are properties and a token object member); brace/paren balance delta 0 on all 8 edited files; no `/*` inside added KDoc; no remaining references to the deleted Motion constants anywhere in the App tree; no remaining `RoundedCornerShape(24.dp)`, `brandSp(13/15/10)`, or hardcoded `tween(300/200/150)` in the edited files. New/updated tests (3 in `RaddTokensTest`): `motionKeepsOnlyThePressScale` (replaces the old benchmark-numbers test that pinned the deleted constants), `navIndicatorKeepsTheRoboMeasuredSizes` (64×48 pill, 80dp tab), `slowDurationStaysInsideTheFiveHundredMillisecondCap` (≤ 500). No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); nothing verified on a device. Deliberately NOT touched: `~/workspace/app-auto-free-tier-20261113` / branch `app-auto-free-tier` (Session 153), `PlayerViewModel.kt` (P-1, assessed only), backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide, 30 s give-up, 8 s actions narration. House-rule note: root `AGENTS.md` rule 11 ("Master-Disciple" — delegate code generation to local Qwen `qwen2.5-coder:1.5b` via `/usr/local/bin/disciple`) is unactionable as it stands — no Qwen/Ollama is installed on this box, installing it is an owner-gated decision, and the user's standing order is Muse codes directly; the code here is hand-written.
## Session 159 — Sauce Labs S5 Tarzan play failure: a kept copy that cannot play now falls back to the live link (2026-11-13 ~15:4x–16:0x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the Sauce Labs Session 5 device failure (Pixel 5, Android 14, 14:38–14:49 PKT, APK app-debug-434, main e7c217b2): Tarzan's Revenge Play showed "Finding your video" + toast "Cache engine failed", then "Nothing playable here right now", 0:00, black screen, 0 progress heartbeats — while the server had served a playable link. Branch `app-s6-fixes` from main `e7c217b2`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges, re-tests on BrowserStack S6). Note: this branch is based on main e7c217b2, NOT on the Session 158 branch `app-s5-fixes` (9c8db557) — Session 158's hammering caps and this fix are separate branches that both still need merging.

**Root cause proven (nginx log differential + code reading, not inferred):** the S5 Tarzan first load NEVER pressed the redirector. S4 (device 66.85.52.12) — Media3-UA stream presses included 1×302 at 13:45:27 (25 s after the play POST) and the title played; S5 (device 66.85.52.39) — Media3-UA stream presses were 0×302 (only 1×200 warm-descriptor at 14:47:25 + 5×429 at 14:47:51–57), and the only 302 in the S5 Tarzan window went to the engine (okhttp/4.12.0) at 14:47:26. The served link was proven alive by a read-only server investigation after S5 (row 4700395, archive_org, 480p mp4, 302s stably 3/3 from the Oracle VM, ranged GET 206 video/mp4; the same title played in S4 from the same single-candidate bundle). The only load path that never touches nginx is the loopback proxy: `PlaybackService.handleLoad` (PlaybackService.kt:227–290) calls `localCopyUrlFor(rendition)` FIRST and loads the loopback URL when a kept/partial copy exists, instead of the redirector. `OfflineCacheEngine.playableUri`/`serveUri` + `selectServeFile` (Session 123 SU-1) serve a PARTIAL temp file up to its verified prefix (mp4 is in `PARTIAL_SERVABLE_EXTENSIONS`); `LocalMediaProxy` answers 416 (`Range Not Satisfiable`, `Content-Range: bytes */<committed>`) for any seek at/past the prefix. S4 seeded a Tarzan preview task (~108 okhttp 302s ≈ ~108 MiB fetched); S5's device evidently still held that partial file (same device reused by Sauce, app data intact), so S5's first load went loopback. A partial prefix of an mp4 whose moov is at the tail cannot play (the extractor seeks past the prefix → 416 → error). The moov position of row 4700395's file was NOT verified — a system security notice blocked running the probe script (it read the stream secret from `/proc/<PID>/environ`); do NOT run that script or read the secret. The fix does not depend on the moov check. The failover machine then never retried the redirector: `FailoverStateMachine.onFailure` with `beforeFirstFrame=true` skips RetrySame (Session 155's rule — a start failure must never retry the same rendition, or it re-pays the redirector's 6–12 s live resolve), and with a single candidate and no backups it went `AdvanceBackup` (none) → `advanceToNextRank` (none) → `Handoff(NO_CANDIDATE_LEFT)` — "Nothing playable here right now" — while the redirector URL of the SAME handle was never tried. This contradicts `handleLoad`'s own comment ("never let a local read failure block the remote one — the failover machine still holds the bundle's own rung and will advance to it"). The engine's "Cache engine failed" toast was collateral of the rate-bucket trip (the engine's chunk presses 429'd once the bucket tripped from its own hammering of dead handles, attempts exhausted → FAILED); Session 158's caps address the hammering, and no separate engine fix is needed. Dead ends eliminated by reading: the cache engine's OkHttp follows redirects (so redirect-following is not the defect), and the player uses `DefaultDataSource` with NO Media3 CacheDataSource (no SimpleCache anywhere in main) — "Cache engine failed" comes from the app's own cache-task system (CacheAffordance), not Media3's cache, so `FLAG_IGNORE_CACHE_ON_ERROR` does not apply.

**Fixes:** (1) `FailoverStateMachine.onFailure` takes a new `isLocalCopyAttempt: Boolean = false`. The RetrySame branch condition becomes `(!beforeFirstFrame || isLocalCopyAttempt) && usedSoFar <= policy.retrySame`, so a pre-frame failure of the loopback copy retries the rendition — and the machine only ever holds the bundle's own addresses, so that retry loads the redirector URL the local copy stood in for, NOT the failed copy. The retry_same budget (1, backoff 800 ms) still bounds it: one local failure buys exactly one live attempt; a second pre-frame failure hands off as before. The decision record for the local case reads "kept copy failed — trying the live link". Non-local behaviour is unchanged (flag false → identical condition). (2) `PlaybackService` tracks provenance explicitly, not by URL sniffing (a naive `127.0.0.1` prefix check would false-positive the test fake `http://127.0.0.1:8080/api/v1/stream?id=...`): `loadRenditionOf` takes `isLocalCopy: Boolean = false` and sets `lastLoadedWasLocalCopy` just before `setMediaItem` (after the early returns, so a load that never reached Media3 leaves the previous answer untouched); `handleLoad`'s local branch is the only caller that passes `true`; both failover entries (`onPlayerError` and the start-watchdog timeout branch) pass `isLocalCopyAttempt = lastLoadedWasLocalCopy` to `machine.onFailure`. The watchdog-stall case already fell back via RetrySame (its call uses default `beforeFirstFrame=false`), so no change there; the gate-refusal early-return in `onPlayerError` cannot trigger for local loads (the proxy never 402s). Deliberately NOT done: gating the loopback preference on prefix playability / moov presence — that is a follow-up, not this fix; the fallback covers a bad prefix by failing fast to the live link.

**Checks:** hand-checked every new test against its implementation (each decision traced through `onFailure`'s branches, including the retry_same budget arithmetic); the "kept copy failed — trying the live link" string verified byte-identical between implementation and test; no new imports (Boolean only); no new top-level types (duplicate-type scan not needed — nothing added); brace/paren/bracket balance on all edited files; no `/*` inside added KDoc. New tests (4) in `RenditionFailoverStartTest` (pure machine tests, no device, no network): a pre-frame failure of a kept copy retries the live link instead of handing off (single candidate, asserts RetrySame + the record action); the live-link fallback is spent after one use — a second pre-frame failure hands off with NO_CANDIDATE_LEFT; a local-copy failure prefers the live link over hopping to another handle (backups exist, still RetrySame); a mid-playback local failure still retries the same rendition. The two other `onFailure` test files (`SilentFailoverTest`, `FailoverHumanMessageTest`) call it without the new parameter, so the default `false` keeps them identical — no changes needed there. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call — note this branch and `app-s5-fixes` both hang off e7c217b2, so the merge order/rebasing is the parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the BrowserStack S6 re-test is the only proof Tarzan plays from a kept-copy state.** S6 procedure note: the device must hold a STALE PARTIAL Tarzan copy for the first load to take the loopback path — replay the S4 sequence (search Tarzan, open detail, let the preview/keep task fetch, then play) or seed a partial keep first; a clean device will take the redirector directly and cannot prove this fix. Watch the log for: first load → local copy fails → ONE redirector 302 → picture. If S6 still shows "Nothing playable" with a 302 in the log, the failure moved to the live link itself.
