## Session 160 — S6 CacheSweepWorker crash: the missing androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler kapt processor (2026-11-13 ~17:1x PKT)

**What:** the fix for the BrowserStack S6 device failure (Pixel 6, Android 12, 16:50 PKT, APK app-debug-449, main c66ec8b9): logcat at app startup said "Could not create Worker com.raddstream.app.data.cache.CacheSweepWorker" (NoSuchMethodException) — WorkManager could not instantiate the background cache-sweep worker, so the daily closed-app sweep (TTL auto-delete + low-storage eviction) never ran. It did not block playback (Tarzan played). Branch `app-s160-cacheworker` from main `7677dfaa`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, merges).

**Root cause proven (APK dex + the real hilt-work 1.2.0 artifacts, not inferred):** the app shipped `androidx.hilt:hilt-work:1.2.0` (the runtime) but never declared its annotation processor `androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler` on kapt — `App/app/build.gradle.kts` had only `kapt(libs.hilt.compiler)` (`com.google.dagger:hilt-android-compiler`) and `kapt(libs.room.compiler)`, and the catalog had no `androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler` entry at all. The two are separate artifacts: the runtime's `HiltWorkerFactory` takes a `Map<String, Provider<WorkerAssistedFactory>>` and calls `create(Context, WorkerParameters)` on the entry for the worker's class name; the processor is what generates that entry — `CacheSweepWorker_AssistedFactory` (the `WorkerAssistedFactory` interface) plus `CacheSweepWorker_HiltModule` (the binding). Without it `@HiltWorker` is inert: the worker still compiles (hilt-android-compiler generates Dagger's `CacheSweepWorker_Factory` from `@AssistedInject`), but `HiltWorkerFactory`'s map is empty and WorkManager's fallback reflection factory looks for a `(Context, WorkerParameters)` constructor the worker does not have (it takes 4 parameters) → NoSuchMethodException. Dex proof from the exact S6 APK (app-debug-449, 17 dex files): `CacheSweepWorker` + `CacheSweepWorker_Factory` ARE present, while `CacheSweepWorker_AssistedFactory` AND `CacheSweepWorker_HiltModule` are 0 occurrences across all dex — and no `CacheSweepWorker_HiltWorkerFactory` exists either (that class name was an early wrong guess; hilt-work 1.2.0 generates `_AssistedFactory` + `_HiltModule`, verified against the downloaded `hilt-compiler-1.2.0.jar`'s `WorkerGenerator`/`WorkerElement` and the `hilt-work-1.2.0.aar`'s `HiltWorkerFactory`/`WorkerAssistedFactory`). That is why CI stayed green while the device crashed: the missing codegen is a silent runtime-only failure, and no test ever instantiated the worker. The worker itself, its `@HiltWorker`/`@AssistedInject` wiring, its deps (`RoomCacheSweepStore`, `UptimeClock`), the manifest's WorkManager initializer removal, and the application's `Configuration.Provider` + injected `HiltWorkerFactory` were all verified correct — the processor was the single missing piece.

**Fixes:** (1) `App/gradle/libs.versions.toml`: new `hilt-work-compiler = { group = "androidx.hilt", name = "hilt-compiler", version.ref = "hilt-work" }` (version 1.2.0, same as the runtime) with a comment recording why both artifacts are needed, and new `work-testing` (same 2.9.0) for the test. (2) `App/app/build.gradle.kts`: `kapt(libs.hilt.work.compiler)` right after `implementation(libs.hilt.work)`, with a comment recording the S6 crash it prevents; `testImplementation(libs.work.testing)`; `CacheSweepWorkerTest` added to the aarch64 Robolectric exclusion (same as the other four Robolectric classes — it cannot boot on an ARM host). No change to the worker, the schedule, or any other code — the sweep is still enqueued exactly as before (`RaddStreamApplication.kt:127-129`: `enqueueUniquePeriodicWork(CacheSweepWorker.UNIQUE_NAME, KEEP, PeriodicWorkRequestBuilder<CacheSweepWorker>(1, TimeUnit.DAYS).build())`).

**Checks:** no local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. Full import diff (after commit): the only Kotlin file is the new test, all its imports are used, zero removed. Duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree: `CacheSweepWorkerTest` declared exactly once; no duplicate groups in the test or main cache packages. No `/*` inside added KDoc. New tests (2) in `CacheSweepWorkerTest` (Robolectric, real in-memory `AppDatabase` + production `AndroidStorageGate` + `ShadowStatFs` fixture, following the `AheadFetcherFinalizeTest` pattern): the Hilt assisted factory builds the worker the way WorkManager does and an empty sweep succeeds; the worker deletes an expired auto-preview keep through the real Room store. The test pins the fix at compile time: it types the factory as `CacheSweepWorker_AssistedFactory`, which only exists if the processor ran — drop the kapt line and this file stops compiling instead of failing silently on a device. Residual risk, stated plainly: the test was hand-checked against the generated-API shapes (Dagger's `CacheSweepWorker_Factory(Provider<RoomCacheSweepStore>, Provider<UptimeClock>)` + `create(Context, WorkerParameters)`; `TestListenableWorkerBuilder.setWorkerFactory`), but it has NOT compiled anywhere yet — if CI's kapt output names anything differently, the compile error will say so and the fix is to adjust the test's factory construction, not the production change. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent's call); **nothing verified on a device — the next BrowserStack run is the only proof the startup logcat line is gone and the sweep actually runs.** Deliberately NOT touched: the worker class, the schedule, `RaddStreamApplication`, backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

**Fixup 1 — self-check, 2026-11-13 ~17:3x PKT (CI 43423437080 red, `compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` only; `assembleDebug` passed):** the test's factory construction was wrong in two places, exactly the residual risk above. (1) Dagger 2.51 generates TWO classes for an `@AssistedInject` worker: `CacheSweepWorker_Factory` is only the delegate and does NOT implement the assisted-factory interface; the implementation is `CacheSweepWorker_AssistedFactory_Impl` (verified in Dagger 2.51.1 `AssistedFactoryProcessingStep`: "we generate a class named `*_Impl`", `ASSISTED_FACTORY` → sibling name + `_Impl`). `buildWorker()` now uses its public static `create(delegate).get()`. (2) `TestListenableWorkerBuilder(Context, Class)` is package-private in work-testing 2.9.0 (sources jar, line 67); the public entry point is static `from(context, workerClass)`. The production change is untouched; the compile-time pin (the `CacheSweepWorker_AssistedFactory` type) is preserved. Import diff: no imports added or removed by this fixup. **Not done:** CI verdict on this fixup (runs on push); merge to main (parent's call).

## Session 161 — Emulator-audit leftovers P-1 (speed carries across titles) + P-3 (player fades ignore Reduce Motion) (2026-11-13 ~18:1x PKT)

**What:** the two remaining open items from `hidden_files/emulator-next-20261113.md` — everything else in its top 10 is already in main (D-1 in 95180a6e + 0950ac8f, D-2/R-1/N-1/T-1/T-2 in Session 154 56fdf505, P-2 elapsed time in 0950ac8f). Branch `app-p1p3-speed-ripple-20261113` from main `14055c0e`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change.

**P-1 [P2] — playback speed carried across titles, never reset, never persisted:** `setSpeed` only set the controller's `PlaybackParameters`; the controller outlives the title (released only in `onCleared`), so a 2x choice on one title rode into the next with no indication. Session 154 assessed this and left the exact change: reset to 1.0f in `loadNow`, never in the same-title retries. Fix, exactly that, plus one hardening the assessment did not cover: (1) `loadNow` resets the controller to 1.0f when the requested title differs from `pendingTitle` — a retry, a resume, or a quality switch (same title, all `loadNow` call sites) keeps the viewer's choice; `onPlaybackParametersChanged` mirrors the reset into `ui.speed`, so the speed sheet shows it. `retryCurrentCandidates` / `dismissDeadlineGiveUp` do not call `loadNow` and are untouched. (2) `connectController` resets to 1.0f when the controller first connects: the service is a process singleton and `PlaybackService` never touches `playbackParameters`, so a fresh ViewModel could otherwise inherit a previous screen's 2x while `ui.speed` defaults to 1f — the sheet would show 1x while the player ran 2x. `connectController` is init-only, so no user choice exists yet at that point. No DataStore persistence (the audit's reset-only recommendation).

**P-3 [P3] — player fades ignored Reduce Motion:** as written, the HUD's show/hide is a plain conditional in main (`if (chromeVisible...)`, no animation), so there is no HUD fade left to fix. The one remaining player fade is the double-tap ripple pill (`DoubleTapRipple`, the only `AnimatedVisibility` / `fastEffects` / `defaultEffects` site in `PlayerScreen.kt`). Fix: `rememberReduceMotion()` in the pill; under reduced motion the enter/exit ride `tween(RaddMotion.FadeWhenReduced)` (30 ms alpha crossfade, the NavGraph pattern) instead of the effects springs. The pill still appears rather than snaps — quick in, slow out — just without the spring.

**Checks:** full import diff (after commit): added `tween` + `rememberReduceMotion` (PlayerScreen), no removed imports, no dangling uses. No new top-level types (nothing added — duplicate-type scan not needed). No existing test pins speed behaviour: the only speed test (`PlayerGesturesTest` — `speed stays inside the menu range`) tests `GestureMapper.clampedSpeed` only, untouched. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict; merge to main (parent's call — a Session 160 gate backup/deploy is in flight on the VM while this lands on a branch, so this must not merge until that gate completes); nothing verified on a device. Deliberately NOT touched: `PressFeedback.kt` (uses `defaultEffects` for press feedback — not player, out of P-3's scope), the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide, 20 s watchdog, 30 s give-up, backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases.

## Preresolve better — the detail-open seed waits 3 s, fills 8 MiB first, and cancels on leave (2026-11-13 evening PKT)

**What:** the user order (21:26 PKT) — keep the detail-open pre-resolve, make it better, and check it really works. Branch `app-preresolve-better-20261113` from main `1d9474d0`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change, no push (parent verifies, pushes, runs CI, re-tests on a device). The audit this answers (21:10 PKT): opening a detail fired POST /api/v2/play 2–3 s later with no Play tap, then on Wi-Fi the cache engine seeded up to 40 MiB in 1 MiB chunks — 36–54 stream GETs over 60–85 s in two BrowserStack sessions, enough to trip the server 429 bucket — and there was no cancel path at all, so a seed started for a title the viewer immediately left kept downloading in the background.

**Fixes:** (1) **Linger gate for the byte seed only.** `PREVIEW_SEED_LINGER_MS = 3_000L` (`DetailPreResolve.kt`): the `playV2` pre-resolve stays immediate (one cheap call, what makes Play instant); the heavy seed waits until the viewer has stayed on the detail 3 s — past the 1–2 s flinch of a carousel swipe or mistap, well before a genuinely interested viewer taps Play (the S6 Play tap came ~30 s after the detail opened, and the seed only needs ~12–16 s for its 8 MiB first window). New `PreviewSeedGate` (`viewmodel/PreviewSeedGate.kt`, internal): `schedule(stillWanted, seed)` waits the linger in the caller scope, then re-checks `shouldSeedAfterLinger` (same release id + still fresh + still on Wi-Fi) before a single byte moves; `cancel()` supersedes, and clearing the ViewModel cancels the pending wait with it. `DetailViewModel.preresolveTopLink` cancels the gate at the start of each load and schedules the seed through it after the bundle lands (Wi-Fi only). (2) **Smaller first seed window.** New `AheadFetcher.FIRST_SEED_AHEAD_BYTES = 8 MiB` — the container header plus roughly the first half-minute of a 480p–720p stream, far more than the first frame needs; 8 stream GETs instead of 40, a fifth of the flood that tripped the 429s. `DEFAULT_AHEAD_BYTES` stays 40 MiB (the player own ahead window, untouched). New `FetchMode.AUTO_PREVIEW_FIRST_SEED`; the fetcher computes the window per mode for both `windowEnd` and the unknown-size plan range. The mark rides `PreviewRequest.firstSeed` → `OfflineCacheEngine.seedPreview` → `CacheRepository.preview(..., firstSeed)` → `enqueue`, which registers it in `CacheCoordinator.firstSeedWindows` immediately before each `startTask` call (AlreadyKept branch + new-row branch), so the first pass cannot race ahead of the mark and a refused enqueue caps nothing. The mark is consumed on WINDOW_FILLED / COMPLETED / ALREADY_COMPLETE, in `keepNow` (promotion), in `handleGated` (auto-preview delete), and in `cancelPreview`; a stale mark is harmless (only a later re-seed of the same source takes the small window first). The seed stays silent, Wi-Fi-only, budget-gated (`mayPreview`) and storage-gated. (3) **Cancel the seed when the detail is left.** New `ProgressiveCacheEngine.cancelPreview(key)` (non-suspend, fire-and-forget — safe from `onCleared`, where no scope survives); `OfflineCacheEngine.cancelPreview` launches into its `@CacheScope` scope → `CacheCoordinator.cancelPreview(sourceKey)`, which cancels ONLY when the row priority is still `AUTO_PREVIEW` — a row the viewer promoted to a keep is never touched, and a missing row is a no-op. `DetailViewModel.onCleared` cancels the pre-resolve job, the gate, and — via the engine — the seed this detail started, using the key recorded in `seedPreviewTopLink` BEFORE the engine call. No Room schema change (the mark lives in a coordinator set, not a column).

**Checks:** full import diff (working-tree variant of `~/workspace/import-diff.py`, since the script diffs main...HEAD and this is uncommitted): added `launch` (OfflineCacheEngine, used by `cancelPreview`), `CoroutineScope`/`Job`/`delay`/`launch` (PreviewSeedGate), the test imports (PreviewSeedGateTest) — all used, zero removed, no dangling uses. Duplicate top-level type scan over the whole App tree: `PreviewSeedGate` and `PreviewSeedGateTest` each declared exactly once; no other new top-level types. New/updated tests (11): `PreResolveTest` (+6 — linger constant 3_000L, `shouldSeedAfterLinger` fresh-on-Wi-Fi / not-Wi-Fi / superseded / stale, preresolve boundary exactly-120_000 ms fresh and +1 ms stale); new `PreviewSeedGateTest` (4, plain JUnit — the gate wait is an injected sleeper held open by a `CompletableDeferred`, so the temporal rule is pinned with no real-time sleep and no Robolectric: no seed before the linger, cancel-before-linger never seeds, failed re-check never seeds, re-schedule supersedes and seeds exactly once); `AheadFetcherFinalizeTest` (+3 — first-seed constant 8 MiB vs player window 40 MiB, a first seed on a 100 MiB file fills exactly 8 MiB, a normal preview still fills 40 MiB; the HTTP layer is a generating interceptor that answers Ranges from a virtual file with no big allocation); `OfflineCacheWiringTest` (+2 — a first-seed preview drives in FIRST_SEED mode once then AUTO_PREVIEW_AHEAD on the next playhead move, and `cancelPreview` drops an auto-preview row while leaving a user keep untouched and no-opping on a missing key). No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict (parent pushes); merge to main (parent call); **nothing verified on a device — the parent device session still owes:** one play POST per detail open, Play reuses the bundle, first frame fast, and the byte counts (a detail left inside 3 s starts no seed at all; a stayed-on detail seeds ~8 MiB, not 40). **Known limitation, stated plainly:** the 8 MiB cap applies to progressive (Range) downloads only. `CacheCoordinator.runHlsPass` takes the mode but only distinguishes FULL_DOWNLOAD from preview — an HLS first seed still takes up to 20 ahead segments (`AHEAD_SEGMENTS`) on its first pass, the mark consumed after it. So HLS series titles do not get the byte cap; their first seed is bounded by the segment count instead. Deliberately NOT touched: the `playV2` pre-resolve itself (kept, immediate), `DEFAULT_AHEAD_BYTES` (40 MiB), backend/Fast_Fetch code, live tree/service/DB, tags/releases, the 6 primaries, 3.0 s auto-hide.

**Fixup — self-check, 2026-11-13 ~21:55 PKT (CI 43466547130 red, `:app:lintDebug` only; `assembleDebug` + `testDebugUnitTest` passed):** lint found 4 errors, all the one `NewApi` — `CacheCoordinator.kt:64` used `ConcurrentHashMap.newKeySet<String>()`, which needs API 24 while min SDK is 21. Fix: `Collections.newSetFromMap(ConcurrentHashMap<String, Boolean>())` (API 1, same concurrent-set semantics; `add`/`contains`/`remove` call sites unchanged). Import diff: only the `Collections` import added, no removed imports, no dangling uses. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI on push is the verification path. **Not done:** CI verdict on this fixup; merge to main (parent call).

## Firebase Crashlytics — crash reporting wired, init before Telemetry (2026-11-14 ~03:0x PKT)

**What:** the Crashlytics retry (the first attempt blocked on the missing `google-services.json`; the parent placed the radd-stream-test config at `~/workspace/radd-stream-test-google-services.json`). Branch `crashlytics-20261114` from main `c1d33361`. App Kotlin/Compose only, no backend change. Parent decision: `google-services.json` IS committed (standard Android practice — the Firebase client config is not a secret).

**Wiring:** `libs.versions.toml` — Firebase BOM 33.7.0 (pins crashlytics 19.3.0 + analytics 22.1.2; both declare minSdk 21 in their published AAR manifests, verified by download — exactly this app's minSdk), google-services plugin 4.4.2, Crashlytics Gradle plugin 3.0.2 (both pair with AGP 8.5.2). `App/build.gradle.kts` declares both plugins `apply false`; `App/app/build.gradle.kts` applies them after the Android plugin (google-services, then crashlytics — order matters, the crashlytics plugin reads the config google-services generates) and adds `platform(firebase-bom)` + analytics + crashlytics. `App/app/google-services.json` committed (project radd-stream-test, package com.raddstream.app). Release mapping upload is automatic via the Crashlytics plugin, so release stack traces de-obfuscate with no manual step.

**Ordering vs Telemetry (the point of the change):** new `CrashReporting` object (`data/CrashReporting.kt`): `install()` initialises `FirebaseApp` if none exists, then forces `FirebaseCrashlytics.getInstance()` so Crashlytics' uncaught-exception handler is installed immediately. `RaddStreamApplication.onCreate()` calls it right after `Brand.load`, BEFORE `Telemetry.installCrashHandler()` — Telemetry captures Crashlytics' handler as its `previous` and chains to it, so a crash is counted in the local aggregates AND reported to Crashlytics. Both handlers chain to their predecessor, so the order is deliberate, not the only working one. Failure is never fatal: `runCatching` guards the whole install, so a Firebase that cannot initialise never blocks launch and Telemetry's handler still installs. No manifest change needed: `FirebaseInitProvider` is a ContentProvider, untouched by the manifest's `androidx.startup` removal.

**Tests:** new `CrashReportingTest` (3, Robolectric): install never throws and leaves Firebase initialised; install is idempotent; install-then-Telemetry-handler leaves a default handler installed. No fake crash in the UI and none in the test. Added to the arm64 exclusion list (Robolectric has no native runtime on ARM — x86_64 CI runner only).

**Checks:** duplicate top-level type scan — `CrashReporting` and `CrashReportingTest` each declared exactly once. Full import diff (after commit) — pending. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** push + CI verdict; merge to main (parent's call); nothing verified on a device — a real crash has not been observed in the Firebase console yet (that needs a debug build on a device + a forced crash, deliberately NOT shipped here).

## Firebase Crashlytics — secret-scan correction (2026-11-14 ~03:05 PKT)

**What happened:** GitHub secret scanning flagged the `google-services.json` committed in `186409d3` (Google API Key alert). The parent's "commit it, it is not a secret" decision was wrong for this repo: GitHub treats the key as an exposed secret whether or not Google considers a Firebase client key public.

**Fix (parent, this turn):** commit `186409d3` amended to `a247912e` — `App/app/google-services.json` removed from the commit, `App/.gitignore` created with `app/google-services.json`, branch force-pushed (unmerged feature branch; the old commit drops from the branch ref). Verified: the file returns 404 on the branch head via the GitHub API, main never contained it. `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON` Actions secret created from the config (base64, sealed with the repo public key — the value never appears in chat or logs). `ci.yml` gains a "Restore google-services.json" step after checkout that decodes the secret to `App/app/google-services.json` (fails loudly if the secret is empty) so the google-services plugin can run. The Firebase API key is being restricted in the Google Cloud console to Android apps with package `com.raddstream.app` (browser task in flight) so the exposed copy is useless elsewhere. The SESSION_HISTORY entry above still says the file "IS committed" — that line is superseded by this correction.

**Not done:** key-restriction verdict; CI verdict on the amended branch (the run on `186409d3` is superseded); merge to main (parent's call); device proof of a real crash reaching the Firebase console.

## Firebase Crashlytics — test executor death: root cause + guard fix (2026-11-14 ~05:1x PKT)

**What:** the `testDebugUnitTest` executor death (exit 1, no test XMLs) is solved at its root cause, not its symptoms. Branch `crashlytics-20261114` from main `c1d33361`. App Kotlin only, no backend change. Commit `b5290dff`, pushed via the bundle route (CI verdict owed).

**Diagnosis (parent, from the CI job logs — not from guessing):** run 477 (TEMP-DIAG 4, job 132245584311) printed the killer: Firebase Sessions' `SessionLifecycleClient.sendMessageToServer` (`SessionLifecycleClient.kt:175`) binds through an `android.os.Messenger`; under Robolectric the Messenger has no target, so `Messenger.send` throws `NullPointerException` on a Firebase background thread (via `ShadowMessenger.send`). It is not an app crash — no app code is on the stack, and the same code never throws on a real phone. The uncaught exception reaches Telemetry's handler, which counts it and delegates to Crashlytics, whose handler records the report and kills the process — exactly right for a real crash, and exactly what kills the Gradle test worker (runs 471/473/474/475/476). Run 474's `--info` log had already shown the 2g heap raise (`e93263c1`, kept — the worker really was at the 512m default) does NOT fix the suite: the executor died again with no XMLs, one skipped class then death 0.1 s later. The heap was never the cause; the heap commit stays as harmless hardening. TEMP-DIAG 3 (single `SkeletonPlaceholderTest`, run 476) died alone; TEMP-DIAG 4 (same test + handler prints, run 477) passed — the prints proved the handler fires and named the NPE, they did not fix anything. Both temp-diags are reverted in this branch (`4475a65b`, `223a1a4d`); the full CI command is restored.

**Fix:** `Telemetry.installCrashHandler` now returns early — after still counting the crash in the local aggregates — for exactly this one frame: Robolectric-only (`Class.forName("org.robolectric.RuntimeEnvironment")`), thread name starting `Firebase Background Thread`, stack containing `SessionLifecycleClient`. On a real phone the guard never fires and every crash reaches Crashlytics exactly as before.

**Tests:** new `TelemetryCrashHandlerTest` (2, Robolectric): sessions noise is counted but not delegated; a real crash still delegates to the previous handler. The handler is restored in `@After` so later tests in the worker keep their own default. `CrashReportingTest` (3/3 alone) is untouched and still pins that `install()` leaves Firebase initialised — the fix deliberately does NOT skip Firebase init under Robolectric, because that test asserts init happens.

**Checks:** duplicate top-level type scan — `TelemetryCrashHandlerTest` declared once, no same-name type in the same package. Full import diff (after commit): additions only, zero removed, no dangling uses. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** CI verdict on this commit; merge to main (parent's call — only after the full suite is green); device proof of a real crash reaching the Firebase console.

**Fixes, in plan order:** **F7** — PosterCard title slot is a density-scaled fixed 2-line height (long titles wrap to two lines instead of clipping mid-word); details line maxLines 2→1. **F8** — carousel dots shrink (active 12dp, inactive 5dp), pill alpha 0.55→0.72, empty-hero content centred, title dropped when there is no art; the Home top-bar search icon is gone (bar + Search tab remain). **F28** — hero pager `beyondBoundsPageCount = 1`, the timing-race fix: the next slide is composed before the auto-advance lands, so the art is there when the page arrives. **F10** — filter sheet rebuilt: sticky Apply/Reset footer under a hairline (Apply no longer scrolls off on a small phone), explicit drag handle, section icons + hairline separators, chip gaps 8→6dp (48dp targets untouched), Language accordion folded by default with the picked language named in its header; Industry "Anime" removed — the server answers `type=anime` and `industry=anime` with the identical SQL fragment (`viewer.py` `_facet_filter_clauses`), so the two doors selected the same rows. **F11** — Live TV country codes become names (PK→Pakistan, IN→India, INTL→International, US→USA, GB/UK→UK, AE→UAE, SA→Saudi Arabia, BD→Bangladesh, LK→Sri Lanka, NP→Nepal, AF→Afghanistan, TR→Turkey, IR→Iran, EG→Egypt); logos `ContentScale.Fit` on a surfaceVariant plate; name/meta maxLines 1 + ellipsis. **F12** — PosterCard × moves beside the title, outside the cleared semantics column (it used to sit on the poster art and eat its own tap); My List shows the "Most versions" row under the grid when there is exactly 1 favorite (grid takes only the height its rows need, `weight(1f, fill = false)`). **F13** — disclaimer text splits into paragraphs, a "Scroll for more" cue shows only while there is more below, the contact email is a tappable mailto link, and on first run Decline is its own line under a full-width Accept button (re-reads have no Decline at all). **F14** — empty search is no longer a dead end: "Trending now" rail (server's "Most versions" order, loaded once per ViewModel; a failed load leaves the row absent, never an error) + "Browse by genre" chips that run the search at once. **F15** — Settings section-heading top padding 8→16dp; the rest of F15 was verify-only (About/version card and "Clear poster images" already exist; no clear-watch-history method and no Terms URL exist — not built; adult-lock entry is owner-gated, skipped). **F16** deferred to after Batch B by the plan; **F17/F18** are Batch C (data verification for F17 is a live check, not code — not done here). **F20-apply** — no token change: every Batch B edit uses existing tokens (MaterialTheme typography, the spacing already in use); the Batch A audit found 0 raw `[0-9].sp` literals. **F21** — search loading state is a `PosterGridSkeleton` matching the results grid (Home rails already showed `PosterRailSkeleton`); `PosterGridSkeleton` takes a `contentPadding` param, and its columns are the shared `CatalogGridColumns` rule written out, never a caller-supplied count (see Fixup 3). **F22** — audit only, no change: selected-chip token pair is 10.3:1 dark / 11.1:1 light (AAA, the F3 token already resolves the contrast complaint); the stored value stays the English name, so preferences, region seeds and the wire never change. Search chips were already row-named (CL-3). **F32** — PrintCard Play/Download buttons raised to 48dp (`heightIn(min = 48.dp)`); Batch A Robo showed 0 touch-target issues, but M3 buttons are 40dp by default. **Detail fallback** — `CatalogRepository` keeps a capped in-memory `postCache` (populated by the list routes only, never by detail) and `DetailViewModel` keeps the cached row as `fallbackPost`, so a failed detail load still shows the poster, title and year above the error instead of a bare "Couldn't load this title".

## Detail regression — Robo Batch B (2026-11-14 ~07:0x PKT)

**What:** Firebase Robo on the Batch B build (APK 484, main `f22b9251`) opened a title twice (works 7681 Scarpetta, 9048 The Penguin) and both times showed "Couldn't load this title / Failed to load details. Check connection." Branch `ui-fix-detail-regression-20261114` from main `f22b9251`. App Kotlin only, no backend change. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path.

**Diagnosis (parent-verified, nginx log as ground truth):** the API answered both detail calls with HTTP 200 and full bodies (06:32:33 `GET /api/v2/works/7681` → 200, 261,555 B; 06:32:53 `GET /api/v2/works/9048` → 200, 130,482 B; Robo device 108.177.29.2). The error text is the `DetailViewModel` catch branch (`friendlyNetworkError` fallback), not the null branch ("not available") — so an exception escaped the detail load AFTER a successful response. The Batch B diffs (DetailScreen, CatalogRepository postCache, DetailViewModel fallbackPost) touch only the error branch and the list routes, never the success path — the cache is exonerated as a suspect. `getWorkDetailV2` catches every `Exception` around the Retrofit call and returns null, so the throw is outside that try: `toPostDetail` (un-guarded mapping), `ReleaseLayoutBuilder.build`, or a pre-update access. A second log fact narrows it further: the `work_ref` bridge inside `getWorkDetailV2` NEVER fired — zero `GET /api/v1/posts?work_ref=` and zero `GET /api/v1/posts/{id}` calls in the whole 06:30–06:36 window (checked with no IP filter), even though both live answers carry a `work_ref`. Both failing works are multi-season series (82 and 126 prints); the Batch A Robo detail that worked was a movie (Kaka Pardhan).

**Reproduction (this commit):** new `V2DetailRegressionTest` (2 tests) replays the EXACT live answers for works 7681/9048 through the production path — `buildRaddGson()` decode → `toPostDetail` → `ReleaseLayoutBuilder.build` — with the fixtures at `App/app/src/test/resources/v2detail/` (live answers fetched from the running server; only the opaque per-link `stream_id` handles and `/api/v1/art` tokens scrubbed to test values). If anything on that path throws, the test errors and CI's log names the exception + stack. No production code changed in this commit — the test is the probe; the fix follows from what it names.

**Checks:** duplicate top-level type scan — `V2DetailRegressionTest` declared once. Full import diff (after commit) — see below. No local Gradle run by owner order — CI (`ci.yml` on push) is the verification path. **Not done:** CI verdict on this commit; the fix itself (follows from the CI failure log); merge to main (parent's call).

The decode/mapping/layout path is EXONERATED — it is not where the detail dies.

The bridge is asserted to fire (`bridgePostsCalled`): on the Robo device it never did, even though both answers carry a `work_ref`, so whether it fires in-process is itself a finding. If this test returns null or throws, the repository is where the detail dies; if it passes, the fault is above the repository (ViewModel) or in the answer the device received (not the one replayed here). No production code changed in this commit — the test is the probe; the fix follows from what it names.
